## READ THIS FIRST

1. What you are building: the `pipelined` branch of `nonarkitten/AP68040` (six stages: IF / ID / EA-calc / EA-fetch / EX / WB, one file per stage under `rtl/`) grown to functional parity with the reference core `apolkosnik/AP68040` (`lib/AP68040` in this tree, branch `e2-fixes`), then integrated into this Minimig behind `rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd` in place of that reference core, then brought up to NetBSD/amiga and Amiga software. `assessment.md` in this folder is the gap analysis this plan executes; read its sections 0, 3, 4, 8 and 9 before M0. `tests/` is the differential harness M0 extends.
2. Decisions already made, do not reopen: keep the six stages; keep the memory arrangement (one shared port for IF and EA-fetch with an IF prefetch buffer, no split I/D cache, the Minimig build keeps `AP040_ENABLE_CACHE=0` and the external `cpu_cache_new` caches); lift `ap040_mmu.v`, the `ap040_fpu.v` datapath and the bus adapters from the reference rather than rewrite them; order of work is integer ISA/sizes/EA modes, then bus and format $7, then MMU, then FPU, then interrupts/trace/STOP.
3. Authority when sources disagree: the manuals win (`doc/MC68040RC25A.PDF` = M68040 User's Manual, M68040UM; and the M68000 Family Programmer's Reference Manual M68000PM/AD, obtained in M0). Section 7 of this plan says what to do when the reference core, WinUAE and the manual disagree.
4. Every milestone is closed by pass/fail checks listed under "Exit". Do not close on judgement. Every new test must be shown to fail against a named mutant before it counts.
5. Hardware is Paul's. You produce a bitstream candidate and a checklist; Paul runs it. Flash only after a passed hardware test, via the Vivado GUI. Never `git push`. Never stage `fw/ctrl_832/version.h`, `fw/ctrl_boot_832/OSDBoot_832_ROM.vhd`, `fw/ctrl_boot_832/rom_prologue.vhd`, `sim/hostcpu-i2c-bridge/i2c_bridge_tb_behav.wcfg`, `tools/vivado/ila_fastram_check.py` (always dirty, two are generated firmware artifacts).
6. Sim legs one at a time, always with `RUNTAG=<name>` set, and read `~/.claude/projects/-home-paul-work-fpga-Xilinx-artix7-MinimigAGA-TC64/memory/sim-ddr3-cpu-bench-traps.md` before trusting any `sim/ddr3_cpu` result (thirteen documented ways it has lied; the tell for each).
7. Before blaming the core for a demo or game failure, retry with turbo chip and turbo kick off (`findings/compatibility/whdload-notes.md`, standing rule).

## 1. Targets, constraints, budgets

### 1.1 End goal
(a) NetBSD/amiga 040 kernel boots to multi-user: full MMU (page tables, TTRs, ATC, PTEST/PFLUSH, MOVEC), precise format $7 access faults with restart, FPU with FPSP support (vectors 11/55, FSAVE/FRESTORE frames), interrupts, trace, STOP, MOVE16, CINV/CPUSH semantics, supervisor model.
(b) Kickstart 3.x with 68040.library, Enforcer, MuForce, VMM, WHDLoad (MMU use), and the game/demo list in section 6.

### 1.2 Integration target
`rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd` (1 780 lines) instantiates `ap040_tg68k_compat` at `:1110` with generics `AP040_HAS_MMU/HAS_FPU/ENABLE_CACHE/FAST_SIM/POST_STORES/FILL_CHANNEL=0/BUS16=0` (`:482-500`, `:1111-1121`) on `clk_cpu` (the CPU island's 37.8125 MHz clock, `:1122-1127`), enable `clkena` (`:1130`, `:1560-1580`), and routes memory through `ap040_ram_seq` (`:747`, `:786`) to the SDRAM/DDR3 controllers and the 16-bit chipset bus. The pipelined core must present **exactly the `ap040_tg68k_compat` port set and generics** (`lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_tg68k_compat.v:17-104`), so that TG68K.vhd selects between the two with one new generic (`ap040_pipelined : integer := 0`, default = the reference core) and nothing else in the wrapper changes until parity. The reference stays selectable until section 6's gates pass.

### 1.3 LUT and timing budget
Baseline (whole design, lib/AP68040 core): 40,950 LUTs of 63,400 (64.6 %); of which AP040 block 28,431 = FPU 11,521 + MMU 1,133 + core/adapters ~15,800. The FPGA is 70 % full including everything else.

| block | budget (LUTs, out-of-context synth of the block) | rationale |
|---|---|---|
| pipelined integer core + adapters (M1-M6, M9) | ≤ 17,500 | reference core+adapters ~15,800 + 10 % for forwarding/prefetch |
| MMU lifted (M7) | ≤ 1,400 | reference 1,133 + arbiter |
| FPU lifted (M10) | ≤ 12,000 | reference 11,521 + EX interlock.  **Measured 2026-09-23: `ap040_fpu` lifted into `rtl/compat/` synthesises out of context to 9,767 LUTs with WNS +14.582 ns at 26.4 ns** -- comfortably inside the budget, and its own timing is nowhere near the limit (it is a multi-cycle datapath).  The 11,521 in the baseline row above is the FPU *plus its glue* inside the reference design |
| whole design at each hardware gate | ≤ 44,000 (69.4 %) | leaves the ~5 % the routed design needs to close |

Method: `findings/ap68040/synth-reports/synth.tcl` (out-of-context `synth_design -top ap040_tg68k_compat -part xc7a100tfgg676-2`, `report_utilization`) with `src` pointed at the pipelined tree; timing check with a 26.4 ns period constraint on `clk` (37.8125 MHz) and `report_timing_summary`; WNS must be ≥ 0. At hardware gates, the full `fpga/openaars/project_1` P&R is the check (Paul's flow, Vivado GUI).

**How much an out-of-context number is worth, measured 2026-09-23 and general, not FPU-specific.**  The wrapper OOC said the FPU would cost **+6,697** LUTs; the routed whole design says **+11,274**.  That is a **68 % miss on the largest block this plan will ever add**, and it is not noise: OOC synthesis prunes what the surrounding design would feed, and it never sees the glue, the wider operand paths or the placement pressure a 68 %-full die applies.  **An agent sizing any block from an OOC number should assume the routed cost can be half again as much**, and should treat the OOC figure as advisory (Q13's ruling) rather than as the budget check.  The routed build is the only number that decides.

**Stop rule**: if a milestone's OOC synth exceeds its block budget by more than 10 %, or WNS < 0 at 26.4 ns, stop at that milestone, write the numbers into STATUS, and ask Paul before adding anything else. Do not trade a budget overrun for a later milestone's headroom.

### 1.4 Tools
iverilog 12+ (`-g2012`), vvp, Verilator (for the cputester corpus replay, iverilog is ~25x slower), `vasmm68k_mot` (`~/bin`), vbcc (`/opt/amiga-cc/vbcc`), python3, Vivado 2023+ with xsim for `sim/ddr3_cpu`, pdftotext for the manual.

## 2. Method

### 2.1 Pillars
1. **Spec.** M68040UM: Section 2 (integer unit pipeline, 2-1), Section 3 (MMU, 3-1..3-34), Section 4 (caches), Section 7 (bus, 7-1..7-68; misaligned operands 7-6), Section 8 (exceptions 8-1..8-29; frames 8-21..8-24), Section 9 (FPU; state frames 9-39), Section 10 (timings; integer tables 10-13..10-28, FPU 10-29..). PDF page = section base + (page − 1): Section 3 base 50, Section 4 base 85, Section 7 base 143, Section 8 base 214, Section 9 base 245, Section 10 base 292 (checked against page footers with `pdftotext -f N -l N -layout`). The instruction set (encodings, CCR rules, EA legality) is in M68000PM/AD, obtained in M0.
2. **Differential simulation** against the reference core: `tests/tb_ap040_pipe_trace.v`, `tests/tb_ap040_ref_trace.v`, `tests/compare_trace.py`. M0 extends the trace with memory writes and exception frames. Every milestone adds programs to `tests/asm/` that run on both cores; parity = identical event sequences.
3. **Independent oracle: WinUAE cputester** (`~/work/amiga/WinUAE/cputest/`, DATA_VERSION 24, `cputest_defines.h:2`). It runs on the Minimig as an Amiga program against corpus data generated from the UAE CPU core that was validated against real 040/060 silicon. Baseline first with the reference core, then the pipelined core; the reference's known result is 3,776/3,801 slices with 25 documented generator defects (`lib/AP68040/doc/CPUTEST_UPSTREAM_REPORT.md`). UAE's 040 is not cycle accurate: timing comes from Section 10, never from UAE.
4. **Mutation discipline.** For every new test, one named mutant (a one-line RTL change that removes the mechanism under test) must make it fail, and the mutant and its failure message are recorded in the milestone log. A test that no mutant breaks is not a test.
5. **System gates** (section 6): Kickstart, 68040.library, cputester suites, FPU test programs, MMU tools, NetBSD stages, the named games and demos.
6. **A round trip cannot see a symmetric permutation.**  Learned the hard way in M10.7 and worth reaching for by default: a test that saves a structure, restores it and saves it again proves the two SIDES agree with each other, not that either agrees with the specification.  Write `cmd1` where `cmd3` belongs on both the save and the restore side and the second image is byte-identical to the first -- the mutant survives, and it survived here.  So whenever a test is an identity (save/restore, encode/decode, pack/unpack, a bus round trip), check **absolute values** for at least the fields whose content the rule predicts, and keep the identity check as well: the identity catches a dropped field, the absolute values catch a permuted one.  In M10.7 the absolute values were all predictable from the rule -- `cmd1` is the faulting instruction's own extension word, `fpt` and `et` are the destination register's and the source operand's images -- which is the usual case: if no field's content can be predicted, the test is probably checking a layout nobody has specified.
7. **Two Icarus failure modes that produce X or stale logic while `$display` looks right.**  Both cost most of a debugging session before they were named, both are silent (no warning, no error), and both are properties of the SIMULATOR, not of the design -- the same source synthesises correctly.  The shared tell is **a value that is right when you print it and wrong where it is used**.
   - **A named block with a local `logic` declaration inside a case item of a `function automatic` makes the WHOLE function return X** (M10.1).  Not the one arm -- the whole decode went X, for every opcode.  Declare the temporary at the top of the function instead.
   - **A function called from a CONTINUOUS ASSIGNMENT is not reliably re-evaluated when a signal it reads from the ENCLOSING SCOPE changes** (M10.8): Icarus does not put such signals in the assignment's sensitivity list, so the assignment keeps the value the function returned the last time something in its argument list moved.  The symptom was an FBcc whose redirect TARGET was correct and whose `redirect` flag was one comparison stale.  **Pass every value the function reads as an ARGUMENT**; that is now the rule for every micro-op builder in `ap040_ea_fetch.v`.
8. **A class that reuses shared phase registers must set EVERY one of them at entry.**  `P_FPU`'s `fp_bg`, `fp_crd` and `fp_stt` are shared by the operand, control-register, FMOVEM, frame and conditional paths, and each of the three has caught a NEW class out by keeping the value a previous class left (M10.2, M10.7, M10.8).  Inheriting `FS_WR` sent a conditional down the store path; inheriting a latched background flag deadlocked a class that sends no command at all.

9. **A micro-op field that another stage already claims is overwritten silently.**  `ap040_execute.v` maps a sequenced stack-pointer write (`sp_v`) onto the micro-op's **u0** address-register port, so a second write placed in u0 disappears with no warning and no X -- M9.T's master-stack update did exactly that, and the symptom appeared three instructions later as an RTE returning in user mode.  Before using a spare field on a micro-op, check what EX does with it; if the micro-op already writes a stack pointer, the free slot is u1.

10. **An assertion written against one microarchitecture is not automatically true of another -- and a DEAD one is safer than a naively revived one.**  Porting `tb_ap040_pipe_compat.v` from the reference core left four interrupt assertions and the IPEND shadow watching signals tied to zero, which cost a milestone's work (M9.T step 4 spent it discovering the core was already right).  But reviving them is not a rewiring job: **the FC=6 rule is the example to remember.**  It said "any instruction fetch during exception processing is the handler's first opcode, so its function code must be 6", which is true of a core that stops fetching while it stacks -- and FALSE here, where IF keeps prefetching the interrupted stream until the entry's redirect goes out, so FC=2 is CORRECT if that stream was user code.  A naive rewire would have made the bench fail correct behaviour.  Three of the five needed restating rather than reconnecting (the FC rules and the claim-arming window).  **When a bench is ported: list what it probes, wire what has an equivalent, RESTATE what has a different shape, and leave the rest inert WITH THE REASON AT THE LINE.**  Named-and-inert is honest; silently-inert is what cost the milestone.

### 2.2 Closing a milestone
Exit checks are all of: (1) the milestone's own `tb_ap040_pipe_*.v` benches pass in `tb/run_pipe_tests.sh`; (2) each has a recorded failing mutant; (3) the named reference programs pass through the trace harness with parity; (4) the cycle checker (M0.5) is within its rule for the instructions added; (5) OOC LUT/WNS within budget; (6) the ISA matrix (section 4) rows for the milestone are set to `done` with the commit hash; (7) STATUS and the log (section 8) are updated. Hardware gates additionally need Paul's pass recorded.

### 2.3 Test file conventions
Pipelined benches follow the branch's own idiom (`tb/tb_ap040_pipe_exc.v`): program poked into `dut.u_l1.mem[]` at PC_RESET-relative word index until M5 replaces the array, then `+prog=<hex>` through the bus model; registers poked one edge after reset release; `ALL TESTS PASSED` sentinel; `string` typed message parameters. Reference-style self-checking programs follow `lib/AP68040/tb/asm/t_integer.s` (`chkl/chkccr/failt` macros, `$F100/$F102` protocol). Name new files `tb_ap040_pipe_<feature>.v` and `t_<feature>.s`; put program sources in `tests/asm/` in this folder until the branch's `tb/` takes them.

## 3. Milestones

Sizes are person-weeks for the executing agent; they add to the totals in section 10.

### M0 — Test infrastructure (size 2)
**Goal.** Every later milestone can be closed mechanically.
**Entry.** Clones of `nonarkitten/AP68040@pipelined` and `apolkosnik/AP68040@main` (or `lib/AP68040`) available; `tests/run_all.sh` passes as on 2026-09-21 (4 red legs, `diff_smoke` parity from PC $402).
**Spec.** M68040UM Section 10 (10-13..10-28) for the cycle checker; Section 8.4 (8-21..8-24) for frame layouts the trace must decode.
**Files.** `tests/` in this folder only; no RTL.
**Tasks and tests.**
- M0.1 Trace harness hardened. Add to both dumpers: memory writes as `W <addr> <size> <data>` lines emitted at the write's commit (pipelined: L1 port B `wren_b` accept; reference: `busstate==11 && mem_ready`, merging the two 16-bit halves of a long), and exception events as `X <vector> <format> <stacked_sr> <stacked_pc> [<addr>]` decoded from the frame words at the moment of the SP write. All 16 registers on the reference side by hierarchical read of `dut.core.regfile`'s dpram contents (the rotation function in `ap040_regfile.v` must be inverted in the bench, not in RTL) so the `xxxxxxxx` columns disappear. `compare_trace.py` compares W and X streams in order. Test: `diff_smoke` still parity; a mutant that drops one store from the pipelined write buffer (`ap040_pipe_l1.v` `wbuf_valid` never set) must be caught by the W stream.
- M0.2 Cputester runtime built: `~/work/amiga/WinUAE/cputest/makefile` with vbcc/gcc for AmigaOS, producing the `cputest` executable and the 68040 corpus (`data040/`). The corpus is generated by WinUAE's `cputestgen` (Windows build of WinUAE with `cputestgen.ini`); if no Windows build is available, ask Paul for apolkosnik's `data040.zip` (open question, section 9). Deliverable: `cputest` + `data040/` on an ADF/HDF image in `findings/ap040-pipelined/tests/cputest/` with a README on invocation (`cputest -cpu 68040 <suite>`), and the list of suites (Basic, IRQ, AE, ODD_STK, Default, FPACK, EXTSRC, EXTDST, FINT).
- M0.3 Cputester baseline on the board with the reference core: Paul runs the full corpus; record the pass count and the failing slices (expected 3,776 and the 25 generator defects). This is a hardware task; provide the image and the exact command line.
- M0.4 Corpus replay in simulation: a Verilator/iverilog runner that expands `data040` slices (format in `cputest/main.c`, DATA_VERSION 24) and drives them through the pipelined core with a flat memory, checking registers, CCR/SR masks, memory writes, exception vectors and frame words. The reference tree had `run_cputest.py` doing exactly this against the reference core (`doc/tests-README` in the reference; the script is not in the public repo, ask Paul whether it exists in `~/work/fpga/Xilinx/artix7/minimig` — section 9). Until the pipelined core has the bus (M5) the replay runs against the L1-array core; from M5 it runs through the bus model. Test: replay the `Basic/MOVEQ` slices, all pass; mutant: invert MOVEQ's N flag, slices fail.
- M0.5 Cycle checker: `tests/cycles/` with one micro-program per (instruction, size, EA mode) row of the matrix, run on the pipelined core with zero-wait memory, measuring retire-to-retire clocks over 64 repetitions; a table `cycles_manual.csv` transcribed from Section 10 (columns: instruction, size, EA, best-case cycles per 10-13..10-28; for MOVEM/Bcc/DBcc/CINV the formula). Rule: measured ≤ manual + 1 passes; measured > manual + 1 fails and is listed in the milestone log with a reason. Transcribe the manual table by hand from the PDF; do not trust any generated column (the Jev-generated "data-dependent" column in section 4 was a poor guess and is to be replaced by this transcription).
- M0.6 Mutant runner: `tests/mutants/run_mutant.sh <rtl-file> <sed-expr> <bench>` that applies a mutation in a scratch copy, runs the bench, and reports `MUTANT CAUGHT` / `MUTANT SURVIVED`. Every milestone's log cites its mutant commands.
- M0.7 LUT/timing script: `tests/synth/ooc.tcl`, copied from `findings/ap68040/synth-reports/synth.tcl`, pointed at the pipelined tree, with the 26.4 ns constraint and `report_timing_summary`; prints the block LUT count and WNS.
- M0.8 PRM obtained: M68000PM/AD (NXP document M68000PRM.pdf) into `doc/`; record its page numbers for the instruction descriptions in the matrix (section 4) as they are used.
**Mutants.** As listed per task.
**Budget.** None (no RTL).
**Exit.** `tests/run_all.sh` green with the W/X streams compared; M0.4 replays at least one suite; M0.5 runs the 17 already-implemented forms and prints a table; M0.3 baseline number recorded in STATUS; PRM in `doc/`.

### M1 — Sizes, EA modes, fetch/vector basics (size 2.5)
**Goal.** Byte/word/long on every implemented instruction; all 18 EA modes; MOVE with memory destinations and MOVEA; JMP/JSR all control EAs; A-line/F-line vectors; VBR-relative vector fetch; ISP/PC from vectors 0/1 at reset; DBcc/Bcc/BSR odd-target address errors.
**Entry.** M0 done.
**Spec.** PRM section 2 (addressing modes, all 68020 extension-word forms), M68040UM 7.3 misaligned operands (7-6), 8.2.2 address error (8-8), 8.2.10 reset (8-17), 8.4.3 format $2 (8-22), 10.4 MOVE timing (10-9), 2.1 pipeline (2-1).
**Files.** `rtl/ap040_decode.v` (size field, EA mode/register fields, extension-word gather generalised to brief/full formats), `rtl/ap040_ea_calc.v` (index scaling, base/outer displacement, memory-indirect needs a second EA-fetch access), `rtl/ap040_ea_fetch.v` (byte lanes on port B, sign/merge rules, (An)+/-(An) register write with the commit-time decision: the An update commits in WB, never earlier; keep the old value in the EA-fetch/EX registers for M6's rollback), `rtl/ap040_pipe_l1.v` (byte enables), `rtl/ap040_pipe_defs.svh`, `rtl/ap040_execute.v`.
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_red_movew.v` (exists, must turn green); `tb_ap040_pipe_red_vbr.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_red_aline_fline.v` (exist); new: `tb_ap040_pipe_ea_all.v` (one MOVE.L per EA mode with a checked result, incl. `([bd,An,Xn],od)`, `([bd,An],Xn,od)`, `(d8,PC,Xn)`, `(bd,PC,Xn.L*8)`, abs.W sign extension, `#imm` B/W/L), `tb_ap040_pipe_postinc.v` ((An)+/-(An) all sizes incl. A7 byte = 2), `tb_ap040_pipe_move_mem_dst.v` (MOVE Dn,(An)+ ; MOVE (An)+,(An)+ ; flags), `tb_ap040_pipe_misalign.v` (odd word/long reads and writes split correctly), `tb_ap040_pipe_reset_vec.v` (ISP/PC from 0/4), `tb_ap040_pipe_addrerr_all.v` (odd target for Bcc/BSR/DBcc/RTS/RTE, frame format $2 address field per case, `ap040_core.v:1946/:2014/:3174/:3274` as the reference for stacked values). Reference programs: `tests/asm/diff_ea.s` running every EA mode on both cores (parity), plus the from-reset `diff_smoke` leg turning green.
**Mutants.** Drop the sign extension of abs.W; skip the An write for (An)+; use 1 instead of 2 for byte on A7; ignore VBR; classify $Fxxx as vector 4.
**Budget.** Integer block ≤ 12,000 LUTs after M1; WNS ≥ 0.
**Exit.** The three red legs green; `tb_ap040_pipe_ea_all/postinc/move_mem_dst/misalign/reset_vec/addrerr_all` pass with mutants caught; `diff_ea.s` parity incl. W stream; `diff_smoke_from_reset` green; cycle checker: MOVE rows within rule (10-9 table); matrix rows MOVE/MOVEA/JMP/JSR/A-F-line = done.

### M2 — ALU core, compare, logic, control-flow completion (size 2)
**Goal.** ADD/SUB/ADDA/SUBA/ADDI/SUBI/ADDQ/SUBQ/ADDX/SUBX, CMP/CMPA/CMPI/CMPM, AND/OR/EOR and immediates incl. to CCR/SR, NOT/NEG/NEGX/CLR/TST/EXT/EXTB/SWAP, Scc all EAs, LEA/PEA/LINK.W/.L/UNLK/EXG/RTD/RTR; DBcc odd-target-before-condition. After this milestone the reference's `chkl/chkccr` macros execute, so reference programs become usable.
**Entry.** M1.
**Spec.** PRM section 4 per instruction (CCR rules); M68040UM 10.5-10.6 (10-11..10-28); 8.2.5 privilege (8-9) for ANDI/ORI/EORI to SR.
**Files.** `ap040_decode.v` (groups 0x0, 0x4, 0x5, 0x9/0xD, 0xB, 0xC), `ap040_execute.v` (RMW memory destination: EA-fetch reads, EX computes, WB writes through the buffer; X flag threading for ADDX/SUBX/NEGX with Z sticky), `ap040_pipe_alu.v` (already has the ops), `ap040_ea_fetch.v` (RMW hold).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_alu_flags.v` (every op × size with the flag vectors from `t_integer.s` sections add/sub, addx/subx, logic, neg, cmp), `tb_ap040_pipe_rmw.v` (memory-destination ADD/AND/NEG/CLR incl. the 68040 CLR pure-write rule, `ap040_core.v:5594`), `tb_ap040_pipe_scc_mem.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_link.v` (LINK.W/.L incl. A7, UNLK, RTD #d, RTR restores CCR only), `tb_ap040_pipe_ccr_fwd.v` (flag-setter immediately followed by Bcc for every ALU op class — the CCR write-through). Reference programs: `t_integer.s` sections 1-12 (moveq..ext/swap/exg) and "control flow", "adda/cmpa", "addq/subq", "ccr moves", "tst" pass unchanged through the trace harness with parity; `bench_loop.s` runs.
**Mutants.** Clear X on ADDX; compute Z non-sticky on SUBX; drop the CCR write-through to EX; make CLR read its destination; DBcc checks the condition before the parity.
**Budget.** ≤ 14,000; WNS ≥ 0.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; `t_integer.s` through line ~342 passes on both cores with parity; cycle checker rows for all M2 instructions within rule; matrix updated.

### M3 — Shifts, bit ops, multiply/divide, BCD, TAS/CAS, CHK/CHK2, TRAPcc, bitfields (size 3)
**Goal.** The rest of the integer set except MOVEM/MOVEP/MOVE16 and the system instructions.
**Entry.** M2.
**Spec.** PRM sections 4 (each instruction), 10.6 (10-13..10-28); M68040UM 8.2.3 trap exceptions (8-8: CHK, DIVZ, TRAPcc stack format $2 with the *next* PC), 8.4.3.
**Files.** `ap040_decode.v` (0xE, 0x8, 0xC MUL/DIV, 0x0 CAS/CHK2, 0x4 TAS/CHK/MUL.L/DIV.L), new `rtl/ap040_pipe_muldiv.v` (forked from `lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_muldiv.v`, driven as a multi-cycle EX unit with a busy stall), `ap040_execute.v` (shift count loop or barrel; bitfield unit forked from the `S_BF*` logic; CAS/CAS2 RMW sequencing; TRAPcc/CHK exception raise from EX = a third exception source, after EA-fetch's dynamic ones).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_shift_edges.v` (count 0/1/size-1/size/size+1/63/64 via Dn, #1-8, memory .W; X/C per `t_integer.s` "shift/rotate count edges"), `tb_ap040_pipe_bitops.v` (mod 32 / mod 8, Z from pre-state, BTST Dn,#imm), `tb_ap040_pipe_muldiv.v` (MULU/MULS .W/.L 32 and 64-bit, Dh==Dl, DIVS.L overflow with V and unchanged dest, DIVZ vector 5 format $2 with next-PC), `tb_ap040_pipe_bcd.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_chk.v` (CHK.W/L N tracks sign, CHK2/CMP2 signed/unsigned bounds, TRAPV/TRAPcc all three forms), `tb_ap040_pipe_cas.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_bitfield.v` (all 8 ops, register and memory, offsets negative, width 32, byte-sizing at a boundary per `t_bitfield_cache.s`). Reference programs: rest of `t_integer.s` (BCD, bitfields, CAS, CAS2, CHK flags, DIVS.L overflow, MULx.L Dh==Dl, shift edges, BTST/CMP2) with parity; the cputester `Basic` integer suites replayed in sim (M0.4).
**Mutants.** ROXL count = size+1 reports wrong C; MUL.L 64-bit swaps Dh/Dl; DIVZ stacks own PC; BFFFO returns width-relative offset; CAS writes on mismatch.
**Budget.** ≤ 16,000; WNS ≥ 0 (the multiplier: check DSP inference, reference uses 4 DSPs).
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; full `t_integer.s` parity; cputester Basic integer slices in sim replay at the reference's pass rate for those slices; cycle checker within rule (record data-dependent rows against the manual's formulas); matrix updated.

### M4 — MOVEM/MOVEP/MOVE16 and the system instructions (size 2)
**Goal.** MOVEM all forms (predecrement with base in list stores the initial value; postincrement with base in list loads; word forms sign-extend into An), MOVEP, MOVE16 all four forms, MOVE from/to CCR, MOVE from SR (privileged), MOVE USP, MOVEC full 040 matrix with write masks (TC/ITT/DTT/URP/SRP/MMUSR/CAAR accepted and stored even before M7 so 68040.library's probes do not trap), MOVES with SFC/DFC (FC output added to the core's request), BKPT, ILLEGAL.
**Entry.** M3.
**Spec.** PRM 4 (MOVEM, MOVEP, MOVE16, MOVEC, MOVES); M68040UM 3.1 (3-3..3-6 registers), 8.2.5 (8-9), 10.5 (10-11).
**Files.** `ap040_ea_fetch.v` (multi-beat sequencer with a register-list counter; the MOVEM saved-EA/`CM` state that M6 consumes is *designed here*: keep the original EA and the index of the next transfer in EA-fetch registers), `ap040_decode.v`, `ap040_execute.v` (MOVEC read/write matrix and masks from `ap040_core.v:1029` and `movec_valid()`), `ap040_pipe_regfile.v` (USP move).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_movem.v` (both directions, both base-in-list cases, control modes, word sign extension, register list order), `tb_ap040_pipe_movep.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_move16.v` (16 bytes, line alignment, An+16), `tb_ap040_pipe_sysmoves.v` (SR/CCR/USP with privilege checks, MOVEC matrix incl. masks), `tb_ap040_pipe_moves.v` (FC values checked on the request; needs the FC field on the internal request = the `c_fc` the lifted MMU expects). Reference programs: `t_integer.s` movem/movep/move16 sections, `t_exceptions.s` "MOVEC matrix", "MOVE USP", "MOVES" sections (these sections need only vector 8 delivery, which exists).
**Mutants.** MOVEM predec with base in list stores the decremented value; MOVEC ignores the write mask on TC; MOVES uses FC=5 regardless of DFC.
**Budget.** ≤ 17,000; WNS ≥ 0.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; listed reference sections parity; cycle checker MOVEM formula rows; matrix: integer ISA fully `done` except RESET/STOP/RTE-full (M6/M9).

### M5 — Bus integration: real memory, IF prefetch buffer, TG68K.vhd selectable core (size 3)
**Goal.** Replace the L1 array with the reference's memory-side chain (core request port → `ap040_bus16_adapter`/`ap040_ram_seq` path as wired in TG68K.vhd, `clkena` discipline, posted store, watchdog), give IF a prefetch buffer, and make the pipelined core selectable in the wrapper. Hardware gate 1.
**Entry.** M4.
**Spec.** M68040UM 2.1 (2-1..2-3: the IF pipe and prefetch), 7.1-7.4 (7-1..7-28), 7.7 bus synchronisation / NOP (7-43), 7.10 reset (7-65); `lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_bus16_adapter.v` header (level-ack contract, sampled IDLE), `ap040_tg68k_compat.v` header, `rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd:1110-1290`.
**Files.** New `rtl/ap040_pipe_bcu.v` (arbiter: EA-fetch wins over IF; one level-held `{req,write,instr,size,addr,wdata,fc}` request stable until `ack`/`flt`; posted-store buffer moved here and translated-before-post in M7), `rtl/ap040_inst_fetch.v` (8-word prefetch buffer with a request register; flush on redirect; the `PROG_WORDS` budget removed), `rtl/ap040_ea_fetch.v` (variable-latency `mem_issue/mem_complete`), new `rtl/ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` (same ports/generics as `ap040_tg68k_compat.v`; instantiates the pipeline, the reference adapters `ap040_bus16_adapter.v`, `ap040_bus_timeout.v`, `ap040_walker_cdc.v`, and the MMU/FPU stubs until M7/M10), `rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd` (new generic `ap040_pipelined`, a `generate` choosing the component; nothing else), `tb/run_pipe_tests.sh` (benches now load `+prog=` through a memory model as `tb_ap040_program.v` does; the `dut.u_l1.mem[]` pokes are replaced by a loader task).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_prefetch.v` (redirect while a prefetch is outstanding must not deliver the stale word; store into the prefetch window refetches, `ap040_core.v:2192` rule), `tb_ap040_pipe_bus_waits.v` (every M1-M4 bench re-run at the two wait-state profiles of `tb_ap040_program.v`), `tb_ap040_pipe_nop_sync.v` (NOP drains posted stores), `tb_ap040_bus16_gap.v`/`tb_ap040_bus_timeout.v` (reference unit benches, reused verbatim), the reference `tb_ap040_reset.v` adapted to the pipelined compat module. Then `sim/ddr3_cpu/run.sh --ap040` with the pipelined core selected (`RUNTAG=pipe_m5`), one leg at a time, then `--ackmutant` must fail, then `--chipbus`. Reference programs: all of `t_integer.s` through `tb_ap040_program.v` (adapted to the pipelined compat) at both wait profiles.
**Mutants.** Prefetch delivers the stale word after a redirect; the BCU lets IF pre-empt a held EA-fetch request; `--ackmutant`.
**Budget.** Integer block ≤ 17,500 (this is the milestone most likely to push it: the prefetch buffer and BCU); WNS ≥ 0 at 26.4 ns; whole-design P&R ≤ 44,000 LUTs before the hardware gate.
**Step before the gate (added 2026-09-22 at Paul's request): Kickstart in simulation.** A Verilator bench (`tests/kick/`: the wrapper per core + 2 MB chip RAM + ROM at $F80000 with the reset overlay + a deterministic CIA/custom-chip stub, unmapped reads $FFFF) runs a real ROM on the reference and the pipelined core and compares the retired-instruction traces (PC, registers, per-address writes). A board test only makes sense once Kickstart runs in sim at least as far as the reference core does. ROM: `/home/paul/work/amiga/Update3.1/ROMs/unsplit_unswapped/kick.a1200.46.143` (3.1.4, read by path). Stub chosen over the real rtl/minimig: it carried both cores to the interrupt wall (2.2M instructions); the chipset RTL would only matter for display, disk DMA or keyboard. Checkpoints and results: `hw-gate1-checklist.md` step 1.
**Hardware gate 1 (Paul).** Bitstream with `ap040_pipelined=1`, `AP040_HAS_MMU=0`, `AP040_HAS_FPU=0` (the core reports itself as LC040-without-MMU; 68040.library will not install, that is expected): DiagROM completes its memory test and reports the CPU; Kickstart 3.1 reaches Workbench (wait five minutes before calling it dead, bench-traps note); `tools/vivado/ila_boot_probe.tcl` for a failure. Record pass/fail in STATUS.
**Exit.** All M1-M4 benches pass through the bus model at both wait profiles; `sim/ddr3_cpu --ap040`, `--chipbus` pass and `--ackmutant` fails; `t_integer.s` parity via the compat path; LUT/WNS recorded; hardware gate 1 passed.

### M6 — Precise access faults, restart model, RTE, format $1/$3/$7, address error completion (size 3.5)
**Goal.** Format $7 access-error frames with SSW/FA/EA/WB1-3 built from pipeline state; the instruction restarts after RTE with EA registers rolled back and MOVEM continuing from its saved EA; RTE validates every format ($0/$1/$2/$3/$7), FMTERR on bad format, $1 continuation, odd-PC address error; physical bus error, double fault → halt. This is the hardest milestone (assessment 4.3 item 12, 8.2.2).
**Entry.** M5.
**Spec.** M68040UM 8.2.1 access fault (8-6..8-8, the SSW bits and the WB status fields), 8.4.6 format $7 (8-24..8-29: every word of the 30-word frame), 8.4.1-8.4.5 (8-21..8-23), 8.4 return (8-20), 7.6 bus exception control (7-36); reference `ap040_core.v` `aerr_start :1715-1760`, `aerr_word :1790`, `S_AERR0 :3008`, `S_RTE_* :3206-3290`, `u0_*/u1_*` rollback `:886-888`; `lib/AP68040/doc/tests-README` "format $7 frames carry SSW and the initial FA" and the WB3 note in the reference README ("restart model").
**Design points (decide, then write down in the log).** (1) Commit point: an instruction commits in WB; stores enter the posted buffer only at commit, so a store can fault only after commit — the $7 frame reports it in WB1 with WB1S valid, and RTE's restart does not re-execute the store (the WB1 pending-write is completed by the *handler* per 8.2.1, as NetBSD's `trap.c` does; this is where the reference deliberately differs — it never marks WB valid; the pipelined core with a real WB stage follows the manual, and the choice must be recorded and tested both against NetBSD's expectations and 68040.library). (2) Loads fault in EA-fetch: the faulting instruction and every younger one are flushed; older ones are already committed; the An update of the faulting instruction is not yet committed (M1's rule) so no rollback is needed for it — only the *already-committed* An updates of a MOVEM in progress must be handled through CM/EA continuation, never rolled back. (3) IF faults travel as a poison bit to EA-fetch and are raised only if the instruction is reached. (4) Double fault: a fault while pushing a frame → halt, `nresetout`-independent.
**Files.** `ap040_ea_fetch.v` (fault sink, $7 frame push 30 words, restart PC = the faulting instruction), `ap040_execute.v`/`ap040_writeback.v` (WB1 pending-write capture), `ap040_pipe_bcu.v` (fault delivery with the requester tag), `ap040_decode.v` (RTE format dispatch), `ap040_inst_fetch.v` (poison bit).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_red_rte_fmt2.v` (exists, must turn green), `tb_ap040_pipe_rte_formats.v` ($0/$1/$2/$3/$7 pop sizes, FMTERR vector 14 format $0, $1 continuation to the master stack, odd PC → address error, user-mode RTE → priv), `tb_ap040_pipe_berr_read.v`/`_write.v`/`_fetch.v` (injected berr on each kind of access: vector 2, format $7, SSW TT/TM/SIZE/RW/ATC=0/MA/CM, FA = initial byte of a misaligned access, restart re-executes the load and not the committed store, WB1 fields), `tb_ap040_pipe_movem_fault.v` (fault mid-list sets CM and EA, RTE continues, the registers before the fault are not re-loaded twice), `tb_ap040_pipe_double_fault.v` (fault during frame push → halt; the reference `tb_ap040_double_fault.v` adapted), `tb_ap040_pipe_fault_flush.v` (a fault on instruction k while k+1 has an An update in EA-fetch and k+2 is a taken branch: k+1/k+2 have no architectural effect). Reference programs: `t_exceptions.s` sections "format error", "address error", "physical bus error", "immediate group EA legality"; `t_movem_restart.s` (needs the MMU for its page faults — the berr-injected variant runs now, the MMU variant in M7). Cputester `AE` and `ODD_STK` suites in sim replay.
**Mutants.** Restart PC = next instruction; WB1S never set; MOVEM restarts from the list start; RTE pops 8 for format $2; poison bit dropped (a fetch past a branch faults spuriously).
**Budget.** ≤ 17,500; WNS ≥ 0.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; listed `t_exceptions.s` sections parity (X stream compared word-for-word for the $7 frames); cputester AE/ODD_STK slices at the reference's pass rate in sim; LUT/WNS recorded.

### M7 — MMU lift (size 3)
**Goal.** `ap040_mmu.v` from `lib/AP68040` behind the BCU's single port, unchanged except for the arbiter; MOVEC MMU registers live; PTEST/PFLUSH with drain; walker port; translate-before-post; MMU faults through M6's path with ATC=1 in the SSW; page-crossing split per page; 4K and 8K. Hardware gate 2.
**Entry.** M6.
**Spec.** M68040UM Section 3 (3-1..3-34; descriptors 3-12, table search 3-21, protection 3-23, ATC 3-26, TTR 3-29, MMU instructions 3-33), 8.2.1 (8-6, ATC fault SSW bits), `assessment.md` 8.1-8.2 (interface list, item 6), `lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_mmu.v:26-93` (ports), `:345-371` (pass-through and the `c_ack` timing rule).
**Files.** `rtl/ap040_pipe_bcu.v` (the `c_*` request now goes through the MMU; stores translate in EA-fetch before posting; `pf_req/pt_req` issued only after a pipeline drain), `rtl/ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` (MMU instance, walker port through `ap040_walker_cdc.v`, `mmu_*` outputs), `ap040_decode.v` (PFLUSH/PTEST forms and privilege, `ap040_core.v:6350-6376` as the reference; `serialise` decode bit), `ap040_execute.v` (MMUSR read after PTEST), `ap040_inst_fetch.v` (IF requests carry `instr=1` and the supervisor FC; an ATC fault on IF becomes the poison bit).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_mmu_basic.v` (identity map, remap a page, U on first touch, M on first write only, `atc_mmiss` re-walk), `tb_ap040_pipe_mmu_faults.v` (WP/invalid/supervisor-only/fetch faults: vector 2, format $7, SSW ATC=1, U set M clear on a denied access, fix-and-restart), `tb_ap040_pipe_pflush_drain.v` (a PFLUSH with a younger load already translated under the old map: the load must not retire with the old translation — this is the drain), `tb_ap040_pipe_ttr.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_8k.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_page_cross.v` (misaligned across two pages, second-page fault sets MA), `tb_ap040_walker_cdc.v` (reference, verbatim). Reference programs: `t_mmu.s` (all 28 sections incl. the NetBSD and NeXTSTEP shapes and the IRQ-vs-MMU sweeps once M9 lands — until then those sweeps are skipped and listed), `t_atcprobe.s`, `t_bitfield_mmu.s`, `t_moves_fc.s`, `t_movem_restart.s` (MMU variant). `sim/ddr3_cpu --mmu` with the pipelined core, then `--mmumutant` must fail.
**Mutants.** Drop the drain before PFLUSH; post a store before translating it; IF ATC fault raised immediately instead of at EA-fetch; SSW ATC bit clear on an MMU fault; `--mmumutant`.
**Budget.** Integer block ≤ 17,500 + MMU ≤ 1,400; WNS ≥ 0 (watch the ATC-compare→ack path: never make a stall decision combinationally from `c_ack` in the lookup cycle; `ap040_cache.v:395-405`, `ap040_mmu.v:365-371`).
**Hardware gate 2 (Paul).** Bitstream with `AP040_HAS_MMU=1`, `HAS_FPU=0`: Kickstart 3.1 + SetPatch + 68040.library installs and reports the MMU; Enforcer and MuForce load and report a hit for a deliberate `move.l 0,d0`-style access; WHDLoad runs one MMU-using title (Trolls AGA with NOEXPCHIP per `whdload-notes.md` is the known-good reference case, its `$7008` fault report is the check that the frame is right).
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; `t_mmu/t_atcprobe/t_bitfield_mmu/t_moves_fc/t_movem_restart` parity (X stream word-for-word); `sim/ddr3_cpu --mmu` passes and `--mmumutant` fails; LUT/WNS recorded; hardware gate 2 passed.

### M8 — Cache maintenance semantics with the existing memory arrangement (size 1)
**Goal.** CINV/CPUSH decode with scope and I/D select, privilege, the `cache_maint_*` sideband that clears the external `cpu_cache_new` banks (TG68K.vhd, `lib/AP68040/doc/tests-README` "CACR enables reach the external I and D banks independently"), CACR IE/DE to the external enables, the prefetch buffer flushed on CINV I, `cache_inhibit` from the MMU's CM bits and the Z2/Z3 windows, DMA snoop path unchanged. No internal cache (decision 2).
**Entry.** M7.
**Spec.** M68040UM Section 4 (4-5 management, 4-6 caching modes, 4-9 coherency, 4-11 maintenance accesses), 10.3 (10-8).
**Files.** `ap040_decode.v` (0xF CINV/CPUSH arms, `ap040_core.v:6333-6349`), `ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` (sideband), `ap040_inst_fetch.v` (flush on CINV I).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_cinv.v` (decode/scope/privilege; the sideband pulse; a self-modifying-code case where the prefetch buffer holds the old word and CINV I must discard it), `tb_ap040_pipe_cache_inhibit.v` (CM=10/11 pages and TTR CM produce `cache_inhibit`). Reference program `t_cache.s` via the compat path with the external cache model of `sim/ddr3_cpu --snoop`; `--snoopmutant` must fail.
**Mutants.** CINV does not flush the prefetch buffer; CPUSH ignores the DC select.
**Budget.** No change > 300 LUTs.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; `t_cache.s` parity; `--snoop` passes and `--snoopmutant` fails.

### M9 — Interrupts, trace, STOP, RESET, M-bit stacks (size 2)
**Goal.** IPL sampled at instruction boundaries with the in-flight pipeline drained, autovectors 25-31, NMI edge, SR mask, M-bit throwaway format $1 and master/interrupt stack switch, STOP with wake-up (and the traced-STOP rule), RESET driving `nresetout`, T1 trace on every instruction and T0 on change of flow and the synchronising list, trace vs other exception priority.
**Entry.** M6 (M7/M8 not required, but their sweeps need this).
**Spec.** M68040UM 8.2.6 trace (8-10), 8.2.9 interrupts (8-12..8-17), 8.3 priorities (8-19), 8.4.2 format $1 (8-21), 7.10 reset (7-65); reference `ap040_core.v:1340-1352` (T0 list), `irq_take_lvl :1877/:1962/:2281`, `:3131-3143` ($1 path), `S_STOPPED :6461`.
**Files.** `ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` (`ipl`, `nmi_ack_toggle`), `ap040_ea_fetch.v` (async exception injection at the boundary: an interrupt is an instruction-less entry taken when EA-fetch is empty or between instructions, never mid-MOVEM), `ap040_execute.v` (T-bit evaluation at commit), `ap040_decode.v` (STOP/RESET, `serialise` bit for the T0 list).
**Tests first.** `tb_ap040_pipe_irq.v` (levels vs mask, NMI edge, vector, SR update, stacked PC = next instruction, interrupt during a MOVEM waits for completion, interrupt while EA-fetch is stalled on a bus wait), `tb_ap040_pipe_mbit.v` ($1 throwaway and return), `tb_ap040_pipe_stop.v`, `tb_ap040_pipe_trace.v` (T1 every instruction incl. a traced TRAP taking only the TRAP; T0 list; traced STOP does not stop), `tb_ap040_pipe_reset_instr.v`. Reference programs: `t_exceptions.s` complete (trace, interrupts, M-bit, level-sensitive IPL, STOP wake-up); `t_mmu.s` IRQ-vs-MMU sweeps 161-164. Cputester `IRQ` suite in sim replay.
**Mutants.** Interrupt taken mid-MOVEM; T0 traces a NOP-less straight-line instruction; NMI level-sensitive; STOP sleeps when traced.
**Budget.** ≤ 17,500; WNS ≥ 0.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; `t_exceptions.s` full parity; `t_mmu.s` all sections; cputester IRQ slices at the reference's rate; LUT/WNS recorded.

**Reference-parity checkpoint (after M9).** Run the whole reference suite (`lib/AP68040/tb/run_tests.sh` legs that do not need the FPU) through the pipelined compat module, plus cputester Basic/IRQ/AE/ODD_STK/Default in sim replay. Record the numbers in STATUS. This is the point at which sections 4/8/9 of the assessment call the core "at reference parity minus FPU".

### M10 — FPU lift (size 4.5)
**Goal.** LC040 mode first (every F-line → vector 11 with the right frame, FSAVE/FRESTORE NULL); then `ap040_fpu.v` datapath as an EX-side long-latency unit with background release; FMOVE/FMOVEM/control registers; FBcc/FScc/FDBcc/FTRAPcc; vectors 11/55/48-54 with the opclass-dependent stacked PC/EA; FSAVE/FRESTORE NULL/IDLE/UNIMP(rev $41)/BUSY frames; FPSP resume. Hardware gate 3.
**Entry.** M9 (needs trace for the T0 list entries FMOVEM/FRESTORE and the `t_fpu.s` single-step tests).
**Spec.** M68040UM Section 9 (9-1 FPU pipeline; 9-20 unimplemented instructions; 9-22 unsupported data types; 9-24..9-38 arithmetic exceptions; 9-39 state frames), 8.4.4 format $3 (8-23), 10.7 (10-29..); `assessment.md` 8.3; `lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_fpu.v:36-125` (ports), `ap040_core.v:4209-4700` (dispatch), `:1364-1416` (vector 11/55 rules), `:4593-4625` (the (An)± update stands on unimp/unsupp; `accepted` → background), `:2170-2181` (pending exception).
**Files.** `ap040_decode.v` (0xF coprocessor space, `go_fp_fline` legality rules `ap040_core.v:6379-6392`), `ap040_ea_fetch.v` (96-bit operand path, up to three beats; FMOVEM list sequencer; FSAVE/FRESTORE frame sequencers), `ap040_execute.v` (FPU request/accepted/done interlock; `fpcc` hazard: no FBcc/FScc while a background op is in flight), `ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` (FPU instance under `AP040_HAS_FPU`).
**Tests first.** M10.0's is written and green: `tb/pipe_asm/fline4.s` + `.exp`, 12 cases through the program bench in the L1 leg and all three bus profiles. Each case records five longwords -- a tag and four DELTAS against what the manual says -- so the .exp is the tags with zeros between them and holds no absolute address; it also carries `noread 00005000`, which fails the run if the core ever READS the operand of an instruction it does not execute. Cases: FADD.X FP1,FP0; FADD.L #imm,FP0 (four words, EA 0); FADD.X (A0),FP0 (EA = A0); FMOVE.L FP0,D1; FMOVEM.X (A0)+,FP0-FP2; FBNE.W; FSAVE (A0); FRESTORE (A0)+ -- all format $4 -- then the reserved opclass, an F-line-class opmode, opmode $78 (vector 4) and a coprocessor id of 0 -- all format $0. The rest of M10: `tb_ap040_pipe_fline_lc040.v` (with HAS_FPU=0: every opclass → vector 11, format $0/$2 per the reference rule, FSAVE writes NULL, FRESTORE NULL/IDLE accepted), `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_move.v` (B/W/L/S/D/X/P moves incl. the vector-55 packed case), `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_arith.v` (one vector per op from `t_fpu.s` sections "arithmetic", "boundary data types", "gradual underflow", the special-value corners 1606-1811), `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_bg.v` (background op followed by an integer instruction, then an FMOVE reading the result: correct value, no premature `fpcc`), `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_exc.v` (SNAN/OPERR/DZ/OVFL/UNFL/INEX enabled traps, vector and stacked PC/EA per opclass), `tb_ap040_pipe_fsave.v` (all frame sizes, both revisions, the `-(An)`/`(An)+` adjustments). Reference programs: `t_fpu.s` (60 sections), `run_fpu_frames.sh` (`t_fpu_frames.s`, `t_fpu_resume.s` at revisions $40/$41; note the GitHub tree's `tb_ap040_program.v` lacks the `FPU_REVISION` parameter, use `lib/AP68040`'s or add it in the bench). Cputester `FPACK`, `EXTSRC`, `EXTDST`, `FINT` suites in sim replay. Reference unit bench `tb_ap040_fpu_normalize.v` verbatim.
**M10.0 — the LC040 chunk. DONE 2026-09-23 (commit 9ffdea3).** `assessment.md` 8.3 already named it: "Decode: the F-line group in `ap040_decode.v` ..., with the LC040 fallback (vector 11) as the first deliverable -- this makes the core bootable by AmigaOS/NetBSD before any FP hardware is wired."  It closes D12 and needs no FPU datapath, so it is the one piece of M10 that can be finished while the FPU lift is still being designed. Before it, an F-line instruction was `CL_EXC` vector 11 with `exc_fmt = 0` and `next_pc = pc + 2` (`ap040_decode.v:661`, `:993`) -- the frame is wrong and the PC is wrong for any FP instruction longer than one word. The manual's frame, transcribed from M68040UM Table 12-1 in Appendix A.5.2 (p. A-6/A-7, "Eight-Word Stack Frame (Format $4)", the frame the MC68LC040 generates for an unimplemented floating-point instruction and which the MC68040 can neither generate nor read):

```
  SP+$00  status register                (word)
  SP+$02  program counter                (long)  -- the NEXT instruction
  SP+$06  0100 | vector offset           (word)  -- format $4, vector 11
  SP+$08  effective address              (long)  -- "the address of the faulted instruction operand"
  SP+$0C  PC of the faulted instruction  (long)
```

**M10.0 length table** (what `lenf`/`shape` in `ap040_decode.v` must learn; `op` = the opcode word, `x1` = the first extension word). All of these are cpid 1, i.e. `op[15:9] == 7'b1111_001`:

| opcode | `op[8:6]` | x1 needed to size it? | words after the opcode |
|---|---|---|---|
| general (FMOVE/arith/FMOVEM/FMOVECR) | 000 | yes | 1 (x1) + the EA's extension words when `x1[15:13] == 3'b010` (R/M = 1, an EA source) **unless `x1[12:10] == 3'b111`, which is FMOVECR and has no EA** (`ap040_core.v` S_FPU_DEC, the 3'b010 arm), or `x1[15:13] == 3'b011` (FMOVE to memory, including the dynamic k-factor form), or `x1[15]` (opclass 100/101, FMOVE(M) to and from FPCR/FPSR/FPIAR, and 110/111, the FP register lists -- **all four** have an EA, which the first cut of M10.0 got wrong and 7c0c905 fixed); none otherwise (register to register, and `x1[15:13] == 3'b001` which is an undefined opclass and plain F-line) |
| FScc / FDBcc / FTRAPcc | 001 | no (the form is in `op[5:0]`) | 1 (the condition word) + FDBcc (`op[5:3] == 001`): 1 displacement; FTRAPcc (`op[5:0] == 111_010/011/100`): 0/1/2; otherwise the EA's extension words |
| FBcc.W | 010 | no | 1 (displacement) |
| FBcc.L | 011 | no | 2 (displacement) |
| FSAVE | 100 | no | the EA's extension words |
| FRESTORE | 101 | no | the EA's extension words |

The only new EA length rule is the immediate source (mode 7, register 4) of a general instruction, whose size comes from the source specifier `x1[12:10]` (PRM, "Source Specifier field", e.g. FABS p. 4-84 and every other FP page): `000` long = 2 words, `001` single = 2, `010` extended = 6, `011` packed = 6, `100` word = 1, `101` double = 4, `110` byte = 1 (the byte sits in the low half of one word). Packed (`011`) is the M68040's unsupported-data-type case, vector 55 -- but it still has to be SIZED correctly, because the F-line/unsupported frame stacks the PC of the next instruction.

So M10.0 = decode the whole cpid-1 opclass space far enough to know each FP instruction's LENGTH (so `next_pc` is right) and its EA (so `+$08` is right), plus a format $4 push and an RTE that pops 16 bytes for format $4. **M10.0 scope, settled 2026-09-23 (divergences D18).** Not every $Fxxx opcode gets the format $4 frame. The split, in the order the decoder must apply it:

1. Coprocessor id is not 1 (and the opcode is not one of the 040's own $F4xx CINV/CPUSH, $F5xx PFLUSH/PTEST, $F6xx MOVE16, which are ordinary instructions): vector 11, **format $0**, own PC. Unchanged from today.
2. Coprocessor id 1, but the encoding is malformed -- extension-word opclass `x1[15:13] == 3'b001` (reserved), or an opmode `x1[6:0]` in the F-line class: vector 11, **format $0**. Validity is decided BEFORE the "is there an FPU" question.
3. Coprocessor id 1 with an opmode in $78..$7F: **vector 4** (illegal instruction), not vector 11 at all.
4. Everything else in coprocessor id 1 -- a well-formed floating-point instruction: vector 11, **format $4**, with the next instruction's PC, the calculated effective address and the faulted instruction's PC.

The opmode classification is `fp_opmode_class()` in `lib/AP68040/rtl/ap040_core.v:1299-1319`, which is ported verbatim (class 1 = F-line, class 2 = illegal, 0 = valid). The effective-address field is **0** whenever the instruction has no memory operand: register to register, FMOVECR, FBcc, FNOP, FTRAPcc, FScc Dn, and an immediate source -- it is NOT the address of the immediate data. FBcc stacks the address of the instruction after it whether or not the branch would be taken, because the fault precedes the condition. Rows 2-4 and the EA rule rest on WinUAE, not on the manual and not on cputest (the corpus contains no FP tests without an FPU); D18 records exactly which part rests on what.

Still not covered by `fline4.s`, and worth adding when M10 proper touches this again: an FP instruction whose extension words straddle a page boundary (the format $4 frame's next PC must still be right when the second word's fetch faults first). The user-mode FSAVE case IS covered now -- it is case 15, and it found a real bug: the F_FP decode did not mark FSAVE/FRESTORE privileged, so a user-mode FSAVE got the format $4 frame instead of the privilege violation (fixed in 458694e).

**Half of this was closed on 2026-09-23 by the cputest Basic replay (cc6c12f)**, which is the reason to run the corpus rather than only the benches: it corrected three rules M10.0 had wrong -- opmode $78-$7F is the ILLEGAL vector only WITH an FPU, mode 7 registers 5-7 are a malformed primary word whatever the extension says, and FSAVE/FRESTORE with an illegal EA are F-line BEFORE they are privileged.  Two of the three came from porting the reference core's structure, which is right for a core that has an FPU.

Still deliberately OUT of M10.0, and listed so it is not mistaken for done: the finer effective-address-mode legality of the remaining FP forms (e.g. FMOVEM to a PC-relative destination, FMOVE to an immediate, the multi-bit control-register list), which the reference core F-lines case by case (`ap040_core.v:3926-4067`). Until M10 proper builds the full FP decode, those take format $4 where the manual would want format $0. That is a bounded over-generosity on encodings no compiler emits, and it is written into the matrix row rather than left implicit.

Where it goes, in order: (1) `shape()` gains the six FP forms above with `npre = 1` where an extension word exists, and `lenf()` learns to look at `vb[1]` for the general form (today `shape` sees only the opcode word, so this is the one structural change to the length machinery); (2) `ea_len()` gains the FP immediate sizes, which is the only new EA rule; (3) `decf()` emits `CL_EXC` with `exc_vec = 11`, `exc_fmt = 4`, `exc_next = 1` and the EA carried in `d.src`, so EA-calc computes it; (4) EA-fetch's `P_EXC` sequencer learns the five-word format $4 push (SR, next PC, $4|vector, EA, faulted PC) and the RTE format dispatch learns to pop $10 for format $4. Step (4) is the only new machinery in EA-fetch; steps (1)-(3) are decode tables. Do not start (4) before (1)-(3) are green, because the frame's PC and EA are exactly what (1)-(3) produce.

Tests: `tb_ap040_pipe_fline_lc040.v` from the list below, plus the Kickstart sim (`tests/kick/`) -- the ROM's `fsave -(a7)` probe at $F80DA4 is the case that found D12 in the first place, and with M10.0 in place the pipelined core's AttnFlags must still come out $000F while the frame it pushed is the manual's.

**M10.1 — the sequencing, designed 2026-09-23 so the implementation is mechanical.** M10.0 gave the decoder the cpid-1 space's LENGTHS, EAs and F-line rules; what is missing is a class to carry a real FP instruction and the two mechanisms that feed the lifted `ap040_fpu`. The lift itself is done (02ebcee, 9,767 LUTs, and the command port is a clean pulse-request / pulse-done unit). The pieces, in the order they can be built and tested:

**(a) `CL_FPU`, and `id_t` needs NO new fields.** This is smaller than it looks. The FPU's whole command port -- `op_class`, `opmode`, `src_fmt`, `src_r`, `dst_r` (`ap040_fpu.v:40-45`) -- is just bit fields of the first extension word: `ext[15:13]`, `ext[6:0]`, `ext[12:10]`, `ext[12:10]` when R/M = 0, `ext[9:7]`. And `id_t` **already carries `ext`**, for MOVEC's selector. So (a) is one new class value and the decode line that emits it under `HAS_FPU = 1` for a well-formed cpid-1 encoding -- no struct widening, and therefore no area and no timing cost in the shipped `HAS_FPU = 0` build, where the class is never produced. The k-factor for opclass 011 is `ext[6:0]` as well. With `HAS_FPU = 0` nothing changes at all and the 160-leg suite is the proof; that is why (a) is committed together with the stub below rather than on its own, since a class nothing consumes is not testable.

**(b) The 96-bit operand path in EA-fetch.** A memory source is 1, 2, 4, 6 or 12 bytes (`fp_imm_words` already has the table), so up to three longword beats on the bus. This is the MOVEM sequencer's problem again, not a new one: a beat counter, a saved EA, and the operand assembled LEFT ALIGNED into a 96-bit register, because that is what `din` wants. The rules that must not be lost, all of them already written down in the reference: `(An)+`/`-(An)` update once for the whole operand and the update STANDS even when the FPU answers `unimp` or `unsupp` (`ap040_core.v:4593-4625`); a fault mid-operand is an ordinary access error whose restart re-reads from the instruction's EA (the CM machinery of D15 is not involved, because an FP operand is one transfer, not a list); and an immediate source is read from the instruction stream, so it is already in the decoder's word buffer and needs no bus access at all.

**(c) The `req` / `accepted` / `done` interlock in EX.** The contract is the reference's and it is already pipeline-shaped: pulse `req` with the fields and `din`; `accepted` rises once classification is past, which is the point after which only completion or an enabled arithmetic exception can follow; `done` pulses with `dout`. Two hazards and nothing else. STRUCTURAL: a second FP instruction while one is in flight stalls in EX. DATA: `fpcc` is a live register inside the FPU, so FBcc/FScc/FDBcc must not read it while a background op is outstanding — the reference avoids this by not dispatching another FP op until its background flag clears, and that is the cheap answer here too. Between `accepted` and `done` integer instructions run on, which is the whole point.

**(d) Exceptions.** `unimp` is vector 11 and `unsupp` vector 55 with format $3, both with the opclass-dependent stacked PC and EA — and M10.0 has already built the machinery for exactly that shape, because format $4 needed the next PC and the calculated EA. `exc_req`/`exc_vec` are the arithmetic traps, vectors 48-54, taken at the next FP dispatch as a pending pre-instruction exception (`ap040_core.v:2170-2181`).

**Tests, in the order they go red.** (a) alone is not observable, so it is committed together with a stub unit behind a parameter — a fixed-latency identity `req`/`accepted`/`done` — which makes (c) testable on its own: a long op, then integer instructions, then a read of the result, checking that the reader stalls, that nothing younger sees a stale register, and that a second FP op stalls in EX. Mutants for it: `accepted` tied high (an unimp after classification is then missed), the structural stall removed (two ops in flight), `fpcc` read while the background op is outstanding. Then (b) with the stub still in place, checked by the W stream and the `readonce`/`noread` keys the program bench already has: 1/2/4/6/12-byte operands at every EA mode, `(An)+` updating once, a fault mid-operand restarting from the EA. Only then is the stub swapped for `ap040_fpu` and `t_fpu.s` becomes the oracle.

**What decides the order**: (c) before (b), because the interlock can be tested with a register-source operation that needs no memory path at all, and (b) then has somewhere to deliver its 96 bits.

**M10.1 (a) + (c) BUILT 2026-09-23.**  `CL_FPU`, the core's `fp_*` port group, the request/accepted/done interlock and both classification faults, tested against a fixed-latency stub unit.  Eight mutants, all caught.  What was built, and the one place it departs from the design above:

**The interlock is in EA-FETCH, not in EX.**  The design text said EX, by analogy with MUL/DIV.  Writing it made three reasons to put it one stage earlier, and they are worth recording because they are the same reasons M10's remaining pieces will want it there:
1. **The exceptions are EA-fetch's.**  `unimp` and `unsupp` are decided before `accepted`, i.e. while the instruction is still being set up, and they are ordinary exception entries: a vector, a frame, a stacked PC and an effective address.  EA-fetch's `P_EXC` sequencer already builds exactly that, and M10.0 already taught it the FP shape (the next PC and the calculated EA).  Raising them from EX would need a new exception-injection path from EX back into EA-fetch -- `CL_INJECT` exists in `ap040_pipe_pkg.sv` for that and has never been used, and the access-error path shows what it costs.
2. **The operand is EA-fetch's.**  Step (b)'s 96-bit operand is read by EA-fetch, the way MOVEM's list is.  With the request in EA-fetch, the operand goes straight from the beat assembler into `fp_din` with nothing in between.
3. **Timing.**  EX's `x.a`/`x.b` sit on the ALU -> flags -> trap-decision path that `m9sc` failed by -0.052 ns and that M11.1 has only just cleared.  Nothing FP was added to it: EX's `CL_FPU` arm is a register write of a value EA-fetch already sized.
The mechanism is `P_PMMU`'s, which is the pattern this stage already uses for a request to a unit outside the core: a new phase `P_FPU`, a one-clock `fp_req` pulse, and `fp_st()` overriding the dispatch chain from outside it rather than adding a term to it (the lesson of `m9sb`).

**What the subset is.**  Only the register-operand forms decode to `CL_FPU`: opclass 000 (FPm -> FPn), and opclass 010/011 with a data register and a B/W/L/S format.  Every other cpid-1 encoding still takes M10.0's format $4 frame, which is right for the LC040 build that ships and incomplete for a build with an FPU.  No `HAS_FPU = 1` image exists, so nothing is regressed; step (b) closes it.

**An FP instruction SERIALISES**, and this is a real cost to revisit in M11.  Once the unit has the command it has changed architectural state, so a redirect by an older instruction -- a branch resolved in EX, a MOVE to SR, an exception -- must not be able to flush the instruction that sent it.  Waiting for EX and WB to drain before the request goes out makes that impossible.  It does not cost the background release: after `accepted` the instruction leaves and integer work runs on, which `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_bg.v` measures.  The alternative is a flush-aware abort in the unit, which is not worth designing before there is a workload to measure.

**`id_t` needed no new field, as designed.**  The command port is bit fields of `ext`.  One correction to the design text: for opclass 011 the floating-point register is in the DESTINATION field, and `ap040_fpu` reads it on `src_r` (`lib/AP68040 ap040_core.v:3898`), so that one form maps `ext[9:7]` there.  `din` and `dout` are LEFT aligned: a word operand is `[95:80]`, a byte `[95:88]` (`ap040_core.v:4262-4265`).

**The stub, and why one property needed a bench of its own.**  `tb/tb_fpu_stub.v` is a bench model -- nothing in `rtl/` instantiates it -- that honours the real unit's contract exactly: `req` sampled when idle, `accepted` only for the long operations and only after classification, `done` a one-clock pulse with `dout`, `unimp`/`unsupp` instead and before `accepted`.  Its arithmetic is 32-bit integer on purpose, so `fpustub.s` says nothing about floating point and everything about sequencing.  Two deliberate probes make core mutants visible in a program: a `req` while busy poisons the destination with `$BADBAD00`, and a stored register whose top byte is `$FF` stands in for an unsupported data type.  The background release itself is **invisible to any program** -- the architectural result is the same whether the instruction is released at `accepted` or at `done` -- so `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_bg.v` checks it directly, by counting instructions that RETIRE while a released operation is still running.  Measured: the mutant that waits for `done` leaves `fpustub.s` passing (263 clocks instead of 251) and fails that bench with 0 retirements against the required 3.

**Mutants, all caught** (`tests/mutants/run_mutant.sh`, new bench kinds `fpu:<prog>` for the HAS_FPU=1 program bench and stub-aware `pipe:<name>`):

| # | mutation (in `rtl/ap040_ea_fetch.v`) | what it removes | caught by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `fp_ok = fp_live` | the interlock entirely | `fpu:fpustub` -- every result zero |
| 2 | `if (!fp_bg)` -> `if (1'b1)` | the structural stall | `fpu:fpustub` -- case 3 reads 19, not 26 |
| 3 | `fp_bg <= 1'b0` on release | the background bookkeeping | `fpu:fpustub` -- same |
| 4 | the `unimp` arm of `fp_st` | the FPSP route | `fpu:fpustub` -- the program never reaches its halt |
| 5 | `unimp` stacks format $0 | the frame rule (D19) | `fpu:fpustub` -- the format/vector delta |
| 6 | `unimp`'s address field forced to 0 | the EA rule (D19) | `fpu:fpustub` -- the address delta |
| 7 | `unsupp` takes vector 11 / format $2 | the datatype rule (D19) | `fpu:fpustub` -- the format/vector delta |
| 8 | `fp_ok = fp_live && fp_dn` | the background release | `pipe:fpu_bg` ONLY -- invisible to the program |

**M10.1 (b) BUILT 2026-09-23.**  The 96-bit operand path: memory operands of 1, 2, 4, 8 and 12 bytes in and out, immediate operands, and the (An)+ / -(An) rules.  Six more mutants, all caught.

**Where the beats live.**  A new sub-state of `P_FPU` (`FS_RD` / `FS_REQ` / `FS_WR`) with a beat counter, a saved EA and the 96-bit buffer.  A read is one byte, one word, or one, two or three longwords at EA+0/+4/+8, assembled LEFT aligned; the request goes out the clock the last beat lands.  A store is the mirror: one micro-op per beat out of the same buffer, `last` on the trailing one.  The saved EA is what a restart recalculates, and a fault on any beat is an ordinary access error through the existing `aerr_st` path -- no CM, because an FP operand is one transfer and not a list (D15 is not involved).

**The An update rides a micro-op of its own** in two places, and both are forced by rules the reference states:
- on `unimp` and `unsupp` the update STANDS (`ap040_core.v:4593-4625`: both faults are reported after the operand has been fetched), so the exception dispatches an update-only micro-op in front of itself -- the same shape a trapping CHK uses (`dsel 6`);
- a memory store's update goes on a TRAILING micro-op after the last beat, so an access error on any beat leaves An as it was and the restart recalculates the same address.  It costs one clock per FP memory store, which M11 can reclaim by making only the last beat carry it and accepting MM_TAIL's rule instead.

**An immediate operand takes no bus access.**  It is in the instruction stream, and a 68040 has those words in its prefetch already (`S_FPU_IMM`).  Reading them as data would translate under the data TTRs and show on the bus as an access the instruction does not make, so `id_t` gains a 96-bit `fpimm` -- the ONE new field, and the design's "no new fields" claim was about the command port, which is still true.  It is written only with `HAS_FPU = 1`, so the LC040 build synthesises it away; `fpumem.s` carries `noread` lines on the two immediates' first and last bytes.

**The (An)+ / -(An) step is the OPERAND's length**, not the instruction's size field: `eacomp()` in EA-calc takes an explicit byte count when the class is `CL_FPU`, and the A7 byte rule still applies to the one-byte form (`S_FPU_AN`: `adj = (fp_nb == 1 && An == A7) ? 2 : fp_nb`).  `fpumem.s` case 4 is the one that makes this unmissable: two bytes in and four out of the same register.

**Still out**: a memory-INDIRECT effective address.  Its pointer fetch would have to happen before the operand beats and `P_FPU` does not sequence that, so such an encoding keeps M10.0's format $4 frame -- a functional gap with an FPU, on a form no compiler emits, and the one thing in this step that is not finished.  Also still out, as before: opclass 100/101 (the control registers) and 110/111 (FMOVEM), which are their own sequencers.

**Mutants for (b), all caught by `fpu:fpumem`:**

| # | mutation | what it removes | how it shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | `fp_beats = 2'd1` | the beat count | the 8- and 12-byte round trips lose everything past the first longword |
| B2 | `fp_baddr = fp_addr` | the beat addressing | every beat reads the same longword |
| B3 | `eacomp`'s byte-count step forced back to `step(sz, rb)` | the operand-length update | A0 walks off by 4 per operand and every round trip after the first is wrong |
| B4 | the update micro-op in front of `unimp` | "the update stands" | A0 = $5016, not $5022 |
| B5 | an immediate treated as a memory source | the instruction-stream path | the 12-byte immediate stores the NOPs it would have read |
| B6 | the frames' address field forced to the PC | D19's EA rule | the address-field delta |

**The timing measurement, and what it does and does not say.**  The out-of-context number for `ap040_pipe_core` alone stopped being comparable at M10.1(a): the core now has the `fp_*` group as OUTPUT ports, and out of context synthesis must keep everything that drives an output port, so the run includes logic the real design prunes (17,030 -> 17,200 LUTs, -0.574 -> -1.547 ns, purely for that).  The comparable measurement is the **wrapper** out of context in the configuration that ships, `AP040_HAS_FPU = 0` with the generic dpram, and it was run four times:

| tree | LUTs | WNS | worst path | levels | logic delay |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cc6c12f (before M10.1) | 30,524 | **+0.293** | `u_eaf/eaf_o_reg[size] -> u_if/fpc_reg[31]` | 54 | 8.677 ns |
| M10.1(a)+(c)+(b), nothing gated | 30,474 | **-0.787** | the same path, from `eaf_o_reg[dk]` | 55 | 8.780 ns |
| ... with EA-fetch's and EA-calc's FP terms gated on HAS_FPU | 30,496 | **-1.240** | the same | 56 | 8.885 ns |
| ... with EX's arm gated too (the tip) | 30,637 | **-0.791** | the same | 56 | 8.887 ns |

Read it carefully, because the obvious reading is wrong.  **The area is flat**: 30,474 to 30,637 against 30,524, a spread of 0.4 % with no trend.  **Every run's worst path is the same one**, the family M11.1 exists for -- EA-fetch's output register into IF's fetch PC -- and in this unplaced flow it is **two thirds route** (17.4 to 18.8 ns of route against 8.7 to 8.9 ns of logic).  And decisively: **gating the FP logic away did not improve it**.  Removing logic took WNS from -0.787 to -1.240 and then to -0.791, and the LUT count UP; the logic-level count did not fall.  A number that does not move when the thing it is supposed to be measuring is deleted is not measuring that thing.  What it is measuring is the tool restructuring a 26 ns, 55-level, route-dominated path differently on each run.

So: the wrapper OOC is an early warning that has gone from +0.3 to about -0.8 on the acknowledge/redirect family, the FP logic is not visibly the cause, and **the decisive measurement is the routed whole design at `IMPL_EFFORT=high`, which is what Q13's ruling says the stop rule is tested against**.  That build ran as `build/stage_ap040_pipe_m101` (the tip 9c93cc5, `AP040_PIPE_MMU=1`, ILA off, IMPL_EFFORT=high) and it is **clean**:

| | m11c (cc6c12f, high effort) | m101 (9c93cc5, high effort) |
|---|---|---|
| failing endpoints | 16 -- all SDRAM | **16 -- all SDRAM** |
| WNS / TNS | -0.483 / -5.988 | **-0.483 / -5.689** |
| clk_38 intra | +0.760 | +0.433 |
| clk_38 -> clk_114 | +0.620 | +0.594 |
| clk_114 -> clk_38 | +0.081 | **+0.005** |
| clk_114 intra | -- | +0.522 |
| clk_148 intra | -- | +0.039 |
| Slice LUTs | 31,359 | **31,851 (+492)** |

So the out-of-context alarm was the tool, not the design: **the routed whole design at the effort that ships has no failing CPU endpoint, and M10.1 did not cost the shipping configuration its timing.**  Two numbers are worth watching and are written down rather than waved past: **+492 LUTs with `HAS_FPU = 0`**, which is more than a fully gated feature should cost and suggests something in `id_t`'s widening is not being pruned; and **`clk_114 -> clk_38` at +0.005 ns**, which is the thinnest margin in the design and is the acknowledge family again.  Neither is a stop-rule condition.  The bitstream is at `build/stage_ap040_pipe_m101/minimig_openaars_top.bit` (md5 9eed78e9671c9f6f95bd09d0b325c68a) and is **not** a gate image -- `stage_ap040_pipe_m9se` stays that, and it predates all of M10.1.

The gating stays in regardless of what the build says: with `HAS_FPU = 0` the FP machinery genuinely should not exist, and a parameter that only half works is worse than no parameter.  It costs nothing to keep and it removes the question from the next person's list.

**One Icarus trap, new, worth the note**: a named block with a local `logic` declaration inside a case item of a `function automatic` (`begin : fp_hw logic x; ... end` inside `decf`) compiles clean and then makes the WHOLE decode X at run time -- every bench in the suite failed with `IF valid x` and nothing retired past the first instruction.  Hoisting the variable to the function's own declarations fixed it.  It cost twenty minutes; the tell is that a change to one case arm breaks benches that never execute that arm.

**M10.1 step 3 BUILT 2026-09-23.**  The real `ap040_fpu` is on the core's port group -- in the wrapper and in the bench -- and FPIAR is live.  `fpureal.s` passes in the L1 leg and at all three bus profiles.  Four mutants caught; a fifth survives and is recorded below with the reason, because a surviving mutant that is understood is a finding and a surviving mutant that is not is a missing test.

**One instantiation, two consumers.**  `rtl/compat/ap040_fpu_tie.vh` holds the instance and every tie-off.  `rtl/compat/ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v` includes it inside `generate if (AP040_HAS_FPU)`, with a `g_nofpu` arm of constants exactly as the reference wrapper has, and `tb/tb_ap040_pipe_prog.v` includes the SAME file under `-DFPU_REAL`.  A bench that wires the unit its own way can pass while the bitstream's wiring is wrong; this arrangement cannot.  What is tied off is listed in the include's header, each with the step that will drive it: `cr_*`/`bsun_*` (opclass 100/101), `fm_*` (FMOVEM), `fsave_ack`/`frestore_*`, `pend_capture`, and `exc_req`/`exc_vec` (step (d)).

**FPIAR, wired at the same time as the design said.**  `fp_ia_we` / `fp_ia_wdata` are new outputs of EA-fetch and the core: `ia_we = fp_req`, `ia_wdata = i.pc`.  `i` does not move while `P_FPU` owns the stage, so the PC that rides the pulse is the instruction in the unit.  That equality is the reference's rule exactly -- every `fpu_iawe` in `lib/AP68040 ap040_core.v` is asserted together with `fpu_req` -- and it is right only for the subset that reaches the unit today (opclass 000/010/011, all of them FPIAR-writing).  **When opclass 100/101 arrives it must BREAK the equality**: a move to or from a control register does not write FPIAR, and the reference already has that case (`ap040_core.v:3936`, the An destination, records no FPIAR either).  The comment in `ap040_ea_fetch.v` says so at the assignment.

**The first program is not `t_fpu.s`, as designed.**  `tb/pipe_asm/fpureal.s` + `.exp`, 34 checks, and **every expected value is an IEEE bit pattern computed outside the design** (python3 `Fraction`, exactly rounded, round-to-nearest-even, which is FPCR's reset state), never read back from the unit: extended/single/double round trips in both directions, FADD/FSUB/FMUL/FDIV with exact results, `1.0 / 3.0` as the one inexact case stored in three precisions from one register ($3FFD_AAAAAAAA_AAAAAAAB, $3EAAAAAB, $3FD5555555555555 -- the extended mantissa rounds UP, so a truncating path is visible), the integer conversions both ways including a negative, and FPIAR.  Integer instructions sit between the divide and the store that reads it, so the store's stall is what makes the value right.  FPCR is left at its reset value throughout, which the next paragraph explains is not a precaution but the only possibility.

**The FPIAR check needed a new expect key**, `fpiar <v>`, because no instruction can read FPIAR until opclass 100/101 is decoded: the bench reads `u_fpu.fpiar` hierarchically, and a build without `-DFPU_REAL` that meets the key FAILS rather than skipping it quietly.  The last FP instruction is placed at `org $700` so the expected value is an address the program fixes and not one the assembler chooses.

**Mutants (`tests/mutants/run_mutant.sh`, new bench kind `fpr:<prog>` -- the program bench with the REAL unit):**

| # | mutation | what it removes | how it shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | `.ia_we(fp_ia_we)` -> `.ia_we(1'b0)` (`compat/ap040_fpu_tie.vh`) | the FPIAR write | `fpiar = 00000000, expected 00000700` |
| R2 | `fp_ia_wdata = i.pc` -> `i.next_pc` | the PC that rides the pulse | `fpiar = 00000704` |
| R3 | `fp_src_r` always `ext[12:10]` | opclass 011's register is in the DESTINATION field | the stores read the wrong FP register: three values wrong |
| R4 | `fp_ok = fp_live` | the interlock entirely | every result is the operand, not the answer |
| R5 | `fp_bg <= 1'b0` on release | the background bookkeeping | the divide's result is the dividend: $3FFF_C0000000, not $3FFD_AAAAAAAA |
| -- | `fp_ok = fp_live && (fp_dn \|\| fp_acc)` (drop `!fp_need_res`) | the store's wait for `done` | **SURVIVES, and that is understood**: `accepted` is asserted only in F_ADDX/F_MULT/F_DIVL/F_SQRTL/F_NORM2/F_ROUND (`ap040_fpu.v:253`), and an opclass 011 store reaches none of them -- it converts through F_SHR/F_PACKI/F_PACKS.  So the guard is unreachable with today's subset.  It stays in: it costs nothing and it is what makes the rule true rather than accidental |

**A finding that reorders the rest of M10, and it is not a judgement call.**  Step (d), the arithmetic exceptions, cannot be the next step.  `ap040_fpu` raises `exc_req` only when `fpsr[15:8] & fpcr[15:8]` is nonzero (`ap040_fpu.v:1887` and `:2156`), and FPCR can be written only through `cr_we` -- opclass 100/101 -- or FRESTORE, neither of which is decoded.  So with today's subset **an enabled arithmetic exception cannot occur**: the gap this plan recorded as "silently dropped until (d)" is unreachable, and (d) has nothing to be tested against.  The same reading gives the other half of it: the arm that raises `exc_req` does **not** pulse `done` (`:1891`), so the moment opclass 100/101 makes FPCR writable, an enabled exception would leave EA-fetch waiting on a `done` that never comes -- a hang, not a dropped exception, and nothing in the core times it out.  **The control registers and (d) therefore land together.**  Order for the rest of M10: opclass 100/101 (which also makes FPSR readable, so the FP condition codes and `t_fpu.s` become reachable), then (d), then opclass 110/111 (FMOVEM), then FSAVE/FRESTORE, then the memory-indirect EA and the remaining EA-legality rules if evidence for them appears.

**The build side is ready for a measurement**: `tools/vivado/build_ap040.tcl` in the integration worktree gains `AP040_PIPE_FPU=1` beside `AP040_PIPE_MMU=1` (default 0 -- every image so far is the LC040 configuration).  `rtl/compat/*.v` was already globbed into the project, so `ap040_fpu.v` needs no file-list change.

**M10.2 -- the control registers (opclass 100/101) BUILT 2026-09-23.**  This came next instead of (d) for the reason step 3 found: (d) has nothing to test against until FPCR can be written.  `FMOVE(M).L` between an effective address and FPCR / FPSR / FPIAR, one longword per selected register in FPCR, FPSR, FPIAR order at ascending addresses, with an empty list meaning FPIAR.  Seven mutants, all caught.

**It reuses the operand path's three states and sends the unit no command.**  `FS_RD` reads the longwords, `FS_WR` writes them, `FS_REQ` is the register and immediate forms that touch no bus; `fp_crd` (all transfers complete) stands in for the interlock's `fp_ok`; and because nothing goes to the command port, `fp_req` stays low -- which is exactly why **FPIAR is not written by these instructions**, as the PRM requires, with no extra rule.  The beat machinery needed nothing new: `d.imm[4:0]` is 4 x the register count, so `fp_beats` (4 -> 1, 8 -> 2, 12 -> 3) and `fp_baddr` already do the right thing, and so does the `(An)+` / `-(An)` step.

**The one deadlock it found, and the mutant that keeps it found.**  `fp_bg` -- the background flag that stops a second FP instruction dispatching -- was set at the end of every `P_FPU`, from `!(fp_dn || fp_done)`.  A control-register move never sends a command, so `done` never comes: the flag latched and the NEXT floating-point instruction waited at `P_START` for ever.  The core does not time that out.  It is now `!fp_cr && !(fp_dn || fp_done)`, and mutant C3 puts it back and hangs the program at the second instruction.
