Leaving it out makes FPIAR read as zero, which the FPSP and any handler would believe.
3. **The first program is not `t_fpu.s`.**  Write `fpureal.s` first: FMOVE.L of a known integer into FP0, FMOVE.S / FMOVE.D / FMOVE.X round trips of hand-built bit patterns, and one FADD, one FSUB, one FMUL and one FDIV whose IEEE results are computed by hand (or by `python3 struct`) and written into the `.exp`.  That checks the interlock against the REAL unit's `accepted` timing -- which is what the stub could only approximate -- and it fails loudly if the operand window alignment is wrong in a way the stub's byte-for-byte round trip could not see.  Only then `t_fpu.s`, which needs FMOVEM and the control registers and will not run before opclass 100/101 and 110/111 exist.
4. **Then (d)**, the arithmetic exceptions: `exc_req`/`exc_vec` as a pending pre-instruction exception taken at the next FP dispatch (`ap040_core.v:2170-2181`).

**M10.10 -- the FPSP on the program bench: 68040.library's FPSP runs on this core, 2026-09-24** (Paul: NetBSD dropped for now, focus AmigaOS).  Commits fdc835c, 25940fb, merge 9efe490 (minimig-pipelined).  This closes the M10 remainder: the `$4160` BUSY frame, the CU_SAVEPC=$FE resume and the deferred E1/E3 frames, and it was found by running the real thing rather than by reading the manual.

**How.**  `tb/mk_fpsplib.py` loads Thomas Richter's 68040.library 40.2 (MMULib, 17.8.99; md5-checked, read by path at `/home/paul/work/amiga/adc/Contributions/Thomas_Richter/Tools/Debugging/MMULib/Libs/68040.library`, never copied into the repository) the way LoadSeg would: code hunk at $10000, data hunk at $1A000.  The library carries Motorola's M68040 FPSP verbatim (the handlers at code+$7C4 vector 11, +$15C4 vector 55, and 48-54, identified from the disassembly against the Linux/NetBSD `fpsp040` sources).  `tb/fpsp_asm/fpsp_lib.s` (generated by `tb/mk_fpsp.py`) builds a minimal ExecBase (Supervisor, Alert, CacheClearU, AttnFlags), calls the **library's own installer** (code+$520 -> Supervisor(code+$54E), which walks the library's vector table at data+$1C8), checks the vectors and AttnFlags it wrote, drops to USER mode, and runs the cases.  The program bench got `L1_AW` sizing for the bus-mode memory too (256 KB here) and an `x80` expect key (an extended value within n ulp).  Suite legs `fpsp:fpsp_lib` + `fpsp:fpspbus_0/1/2`, run in parallel, ~80 s; skipped with a message if the library is absent.

**What is verified -- 278 checks, all pass, L1 and all three bus profiles:**
- **Unimplemented instructions (vector 11, the $4130 frame):** FSIN (0.5, 1, -2.5, 100, 1e-5), FCOS (0.5, 2, 1000), FTAN, FASIN, FACOS, FATAN (2, -0.5), FSINH, FCOSH, FTANH, FATANH, FETOX (1, -3.5, 10), FETOXM1, FTWOTOX, FTENTOX, FLOGN (2, 10, 0.3), FLOGNP1, FLOG10, FLOG2, FGETEXP, FGETMAN, FINT (RNE ties both ways), FINTRZ, FSINCOS, FMOD / FREM (with the FPSR quotient byte), FSCALE, FMOVECR at eleven ROM offsets; source forms FPn, extended memory, double / single / long / word immediate, `(An)+` and `-(An)` (An already stepped when the FPSP runs).  Pass A (extended) checks every transcendental **bit-exactly** against `tb/fpsp_model` -- Previous's `softfloat_fpsp.c`, an independent C transcription of the FPSP algorithms -- and the generator refuses a model value more than 32 ulp from mpmath's; pass B (FPCR = double) checks 34 results **exactly against the correctly rounded double** from mpmath (none within 2^-10 ulp of a midpoint).  FPSR condition codes on every case; the full FPSR where the rule is simple.
- **Unsupported data types (vector 55, the $4160 BUSY frame):** FMOVE.P in (immediate and memory, exact and 1-ulp values), FMOVE.P out with a static k-factor (3 cases, exact packed images), an extended denormal into a register and back out, a denormal register operand to FMUL (which needs the CU_SAVEPC=$FE resume), a double-denormal and a single-denormal source.
- **Findings about the FPSP itself, not the core** (the model shows the same digits): its REDUCEX is outside the documented 1 ulp at large arguments -- sin(100) 2 ulp, cos(1000) 19 ulp -- and FMOVECR #$0C (e) returns Motorola's table value $ADF85458_A2BB4A9A, one below the exactly rounded one, which is what a real 68040 returns.  And the Motorola extended denormal convention: exponent field 0 means 2^-16383 with the explicit integer bit (x87 would say -16382); the FPSP's nrm_set and the unit agree.

**Core and unit bugs it found and fixed (test first, named mutants):**
1. **FRESTORE refused the $4160 frame** (format error on the FPSP's first vector-55 exit: `gen_except.sa do_clean` rebuilds a BUSY frame and FRESTOREs it) and **FSAVE wrote the unit's BUSY state as a $4130 frame**.  Now 25 longwords both ways in lib/AP68040's `fsave_busy_word` layout (Motorola fpsp.h offsets), `-(An)` walking back 96 more, `(An)+` stepping 100 (the `an_ov` hook, as M10.7 foresaw).
2. **No CU_SAVEPC=$FE resume**: a restored BUSY frame must execute its command from ETEMP/FPTEMP (`res_func.sa fix_stk`).  Ported from apolkosnik/AP68040 880b81c into the lifted unit (F_RESTORE_A/B/N, ETE15/FPTE15, signed working exponents; not its regfile restructure); the core releases it like a background operation (`fp_bg`).  Upstream's own `t_fpu_resume.s` (57 cases) passes on this core and is now a compat leg.
3. **The packed-decimal source payload was all zeros** (FMOVE.P in came back 0.0): upstream a8a50ce's split across ETEMP/FPTEMP, ported; with it upstream's FPIARCU same-edge fix.
4. **Single/double denormal sources left the raw memory image in ETEMP** (a single's fraction sat in bits 95:64, where the FPSP never looks: 2^-127 came back as a negative unnormal).  Now 0.fraction under WinUAE's exponent word $3F80/$3C00 (D24).  Upstream has the same bug.
5. **The deferred exception's frame** (the M10.5 / M10.3 gap): a released operation's pending exception now pulses `pend_capture`; FSAVE extracts the prepared frame instead of taking the exception, FRESTORE no longer takes it (NULL/IDLE discard it, an E1/E3 frame re-arms it) -- D25.  Upstream's `t_fpu_frames.s` failed at test 30 before and passes now (compat leg).
Tests: `tb/pipe_asm/fpureal_busy.s` (116 checks: the frame absolutely, the round trip, both An steps, two resumes, packed and S/D-denormal payloads, a NULL FRESTORE discarding a pending INEX2), `tb/fpsp_asm/fpsp_lib.s`, upstream's t_fpu_resume/t_fpu_frames.  **Mutants (`tb/fpsp_mutants.sh`), 18, all caught:** BZ1 FSAVE ignores BUSY, BZ2 the -(An) walk 48, BZ3 the (An)+ step 52, BZ4 $4160 refused, BZ5 resume not released, BZ6 CU_SAVEPC not captured, BZ7 ETE15 not captured, BZ8 FPIARCU not captured, BU1 resume off, BU2 the resumed destination reloaded from the register, BU3 ETE15 shift ignored, BU4 packed payload zero, BU5 S/D denormal raw image, BU6 FPIARCU of the previous instruction, PC1 no pend_capture, PC2 FSAVE takes a framed pend, PC3 FRESTORE does not re-arm, PC4 FRESTORE takes the pend.  LC040 Kickstart leg 226dee9c.../26,935,272 cycles and the FPU leg (0883f09d..., 2,289,114 insns / 26,939,749 cycles) are both bit-identical to 629eab9.

**The reference's `t_fpu.s` as a cross-check** (compat bench, not in the suite, because it still stops): at 629eab9 it stopped at test 184 -- its OWN check that a denormal source's FSAVE is the $41/$60 BUSY frame, "not IDLE".  At 25940fb it passes 184 and runs on to test 241 in §47, `fdiv.w ([$3740.w]),fp5`: a MEMORY-INDIRECT FP effective address, M10.1(b)'s recorded gap (it takes M10.0's format $4 frame; `--diag` reads $402C).  §47's tail and §48-50 are therefore still unreached through `t_fpu.s` (they cannot be skipped past: §47 defines symbols the rest uses), but upstream's `t_fpu_frames.s` covers the same state-frame lifecycle and passes.

**What is still NOT verified here**, so nobody reads more into this than it says: enabled arithmetic exceptions routed through the FPSP's own handlers (68040.library sends vectors 48-54 to the OS's previous handler unless FPUControl disables them, and AmigaOS runs with them disabled); FMOVE.P out with a DYNAMIC k-factor and the packed exponent's fourth digit; the FPSP in supervisor mode (the program runs in user mode; the handlers' MOVES path is the one exercised); a store's enabled exception (M10.3's gap, still open); `t_fpu.s` §48-50 (not re-run).  No FPU image with this work has been built -- that needs Vivado, which this session did not touch.

**Mutants.** (An)+ update withheld on unimp; `fpcc` read while background op pending; IDLE frame written as 24 bytes; vector 55 stacks format $2; (M10.0) format $0 instead of $4; `next_pc` = pc + 2 for a multi-word FP instruction; the EA longword left out so RTE pops 12.
**Budget.** FPU ≤ 12,000 on top of the integer block; whole-design P&R ≤ 44,000 (this is the milestone where the stop rule is most likely to trigger; if it does, ask Paul whether the FPU stays optional in the openaars build).
**Hardware gate 3 (Paul).** `HAS_FPU=1` bitstream: Kickstart + 68040.library reports FPU; the FPU test programs (section 6.2) pass; a demo using the FPU from section 6 runs.
**Exit.** Benches + mutants; `t_fpu.s`, `t_fpu_frames.s`, `t_fpu_resume.s` parity; cputester FP suites at the reference's rate in sim; LUT/WNS recorded; hardware gate 3 passed.

### M11 — Throughput and timing closure (size 2)
**Goal.** Cycle checker within rule for every matrix row; no unexplained bubbles; WNS ≥ 0 in full P&R at the CPU clock; profile of IF/EA-fetch port conflicts to decide M14.
**Entry.** M10 (or M9 if the FPU is deferred by the stop rule).
**Spec.** M68040UM 2.1 (2-1..2-3), Section 10 in full.
**Files.** Any stage; `tests/cycles/`.
**Tests.** The cycle checker over the complete matrix; `bench_loop.s` and `dhry` -- **`dhry` DOES NOT EXIST in this tree (checked 2026-09-23: `lib/AP68040/tb/build_tests.sh` has no Dhrystone target and `tb/build/` has no such image), so do not go looking for it; `bench_loop.s` is the stand-in and the measurement pass used it** -- retire-count comparison against the reference core (the pipelined core must retire more instructions per clock on both; record the ratio); the `sim/ddr3_cpu` placement histogram must stay on the board grid (bench-traps 5-7). Port-conflict profile: count cycles IF is stalled by an EA-fetch request over `dhry`; if > 15 % of cycles, M14 is justified.
**M11.1 -- the acknowledge-to-IF family (written 2026-09-23, from the m9sc/m9sd builds).** This is the one structural timing problem the core has, and it is worth stating before anyone tries again to fix it locally.

TG68K registers the memory acknowledge (`x_ack_r`) on the clk_114 edge *immediately before* the core's clk_38 edge -- that is the whole point of the handshake, and `cpu.xdc` deliberately refuses to relax the crossing (its own words: "Leaving the default is not a compromise for those: it is 8.815 ns, which is exactly the requirement they meet today"). So everything from the acknowledge to a core register has ONE clk_114 period.

EA-fetch's redirect decision (`eaf_redir_v`, `redirect_pc`) is computed from that acknowledge, because a load's answer can be what completes a redirecting instruction -- RTS, RTE, JMP (An), an exception's vector fetch -- and the core redirects in the same clock the data arrives so as not to lose one. From there the redirect reaches IF's fetch PC, IF's queue, ID's gather and EA-calc, which is a wide fan-out inside those 8.815 ns.

Two attempts have now been measured and both are recorded so they are not repeated:
- 9d6cba7 / 230ba47 (M9 subset): taking the interrupt out of the chain and making it a register cut 180 failing endpoints down to 6. That worked.
- ab95926 (2026-09-23): taking IF's 32-bit fetch-PC ADDER off the redirect path made the whole design far worse -- 182 failing endpoints against 22 -- because a cheap D let the tools move the acknowledge-derived redirect condition onto the CLOCK ENABLES of the prefetch queue, ~100 pins of fan-out. Reverted. **A smaller D is not automatically a shorter path**; measure the whole design, and list the failing paths with `tests/synth/failing_paths.tcl` rather than reading the summary's one-path-per-group.

**Tried 2026-09-23, and the result is a parameter, not a decision.**  `REDIR_REG` (commit 378f888, **default 0 so nothing changes**) holds EA-fetch's redirect in a register for one clock.  In the gap clock the stage does nothing -- `eac_v_use` is low so stepf sees no instruction, `eaf_stall` keeps the real `eac_valid` and holds EA-calc, and the instruction EA-calc took meanwhile is killed by `flush_eac` and never arrives.  With it OFF the suite is 160 legs green (that is what makes the commit safe).  **With it ON, 159 of 160 pass** -- every pipe_asm program in the L1 leg and all three bus profiles, every MMU and interrupt program through the wrapper.  The single failure is `compat:reset`, "access outside memory model at 0000841a": with the redirect a clock late, IF issues ONE more speculative fetch down the old stream before it turns, and that bench's model is tight enough to see it.  The fetch is architecturally harmless (a real 68040 prefetches well past an RTS, the answer is dropped by the existing `fdrop` path, and a faulting one is only raised if the word is used) but it IS a change in the core's memory footprint, so it was not waved through by relaxing the bench.  **Settled the same night (c676cc8): IF is held for the gap clock.**  EA-fetch exports `eaf_redir_soon` -- the clock before the held redirect goes out -- and the core ORs it into IF's existing `fetch_hold`, the input PTEST/PFLUSH already uses.  The extra speculative fetch is gone and **the suite is 160 of 160 green with REDIR_REG = 1**, `compat:reset` included.  The acknowledge does still reach IF through this, but as one term of the fetch REQUEST rather than as the clock enable of the whole prefetch queue, which is the thing that made ab95926 ten times worse.  **The routed build settles it, and it is a decisive win.**  `stage_ap040_pipe_m11a`, DEFAULT effort so it compares like for like with `stage_ap040_pipe_m9sc`:

| | m9sc (REDIR_REG=0) | m11a (REDIR_REG=1) |
|---|---|---|
| failing endpoints | 22 | **16 -- every one the SDRAM path** |
| clk_114 -> clk_38 | **-0.075**, 3 endpoints | **+0.032**, none |
| clk_38 intra | **-0.052**, 4 endpoints | **+0.424**, none |
| clk_148 intra | -0.014, 1 endpoint | +0.127, none |
| Slice LUTs | 30,974 | 31,362 (+388) |

So the held redirect closes the acknowledge family **at the default effort** -- the design no longer needs `IMPL_EFFORT=high` to meet its CPU paths -- and it takes the clk_38-intra family with it.  That is the headroom M10's FPU will need.

**What it costs is one clock** on RTS/RTD/RTR, a memory-indirect JMP/JSR and a wrongly guessed Bcc/DBcc -- and that has now been measured on a real workload, not guessed.  The Kickstart bench runs the same 2,288,738 instructions of a 3.1.4 boot either way:

| core | instructions | clocks | vs the reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| reference AP68040 | 2,289,114 | 33,496,750 | 1.000 |
| pipelined, REDIR_REG = 0 | 2,288,738 | 26,724,601 | 0.798 |
| pipelined, REDIR_REG = 1 | 2,288,738 | **26,758,312** | **0.799** |

**33,711 clocks, +0.126 %.** That is what the whole acknowledge timing family costs to close, on a workload that is all of exec, intuition, trackdisk and the ROM's init -- exactly the RTS-heavy code the change taxes.  The pipelined core is still 0.80x the reference's clocks.

The parameter is therefore left ON at the tip (31735c5) with the measurement recorded, and **Q14 asks Paul to confirm the trade** rather than assuming it.  It changes nothing about the image he loads: that is `stage_ap040_pipe_m9se`, built from 230ba47, which predates all of it.

The options, in the order they should be tried:
1. **Register the redirect.** EA-fetch latches "a redirect is due, to this PC" in the clock the acknowledge arrives and issues it the next clock. The acknowledge then reaches exactly one register and nothing else. Cost: one clock on every redirect that depends on a load -- RTS, RTE, JMP/JSR (An) and every exception's vector fetch. Measure it with `bench_loop.s` and `dhry` before and after; the cycle checker's RTS/JMP rows move by one, which is inside the "measured <= manual + 1" rule.
2. **An early acknowledge from the wrapper.** The SDRAM and DDR3 sequencers know a clk_114 cycle in advance that data will land. A `will_ack` one cycle early would give the core 17.6 ns instead of 8.8 for the decision, with no lost clock. It is a change in `rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd` and `ap040_ram_seq.vhd`, not in the core, so it needs Paul: the same wrapper serves the reference core.
3. Relaxing the crossing with a multicycle exception is NOT an option: `cpu.xdc` derives why it would be false.

**Mutants.** n/a (measurement milestone), but every RTL change made here re-runs the full pipe suite and the parity checkpoint.
**Budget.** As M10.
**Exit.** Checker table with zero rows over rule (or each listed with a manual citation for why the 040 number is unreachable in this design, and Paul's sign-off); IPC ratio recorded; WNS ≥ 0 in full P&R; profile number recorded.

**M11.2 -- MEASURED FINDING (2026-09-24): why SysInfo's SPEED says 0.33x, and why the TG68K says 0.50x.**

*The workload.* SysInfo 4.4 is on disk (`/home/paul/work/amiga/SysInfo.lha`), so the real loop was extracted rather than imitated: `tests/perf/mk_siloop.py` reads hunk 0 of the executable and takes the SPEED routine at `$3068` -- a loop that counts how many passes of a fixed body complete in 200 VBlanks (a VERTB server increments `$480C`), then divides by the machine constants at `$2B88..$2BD4` (`$4746` = 18,246 passes/s is the A4000/040-25 row, the last and largest).  One pass is **847 instructions**: 54 BSR + 54 RTS (15 outer calls, 40 nested BSR/RTS pairs inside the 8x-unrolled block at `$3154`), 167 MOVE.L (mostly 6-byte immediates), 160 ADD.L, 40 each of ADDI/SUBI/EXT.W/EXT.L/SWAP/EXG/CLR/NEG, 120 MOVE.L d(A0),D0 loads, one each of MULS.W/MULU.W/DIVS.W/DIVU.W, four shifts/rotates, five AND/OR/EOR/NOT groups.  Three size-preserving patches only: the VBlank compare becomes an iteration compare (`cmp.l (a4),d7 / blt`, the memory read stays), the RemIntServer after the loop becomes RTS, and the eight absolute `#$45C4` table pointers are relocated.  The block keeps SysInfo's cache-line alignment.

*Calibration.* With 18,246 passes/s = 1.00: TG68K 0.50x = 109.6 us/pass; pipelined 0.33x = 166 us/pass = **~6,280 clk_38**; the reference AP68040 on the board was 0.28x (D3/E2 logs, `findings/ap68040/stage-e/e2-results.md`).

*The TG68K's clock, from the RTL (tag d3_stable, rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd g_tg68k; rtl/sdram/cpu_enable_cadence.v): CONFIRMED.* TG68KdotC_Kernel is clocked by `clk` = clk_114 = 113.4375 MHz with `clkena = clkena_in AND bus_release`, and `clkena_in` is sdram_ctrl's `enaWRreg`, high on phases 2/6/10/14 of the 16-phase round -- so at most one advance in four clocks, **28.36 MHz**, less during bus waits.  0.50x is therefore **~3,110 TG68K advances per pass, 3.7 per instruction**.  The pipelined core at 37.8125 MHz spends **~7.4 clk_38 per instruction** on the same loop.

*Where the pipelined core's clocks go -- measured with a cycle-accounting probe (`tb/perf/perf_probe.vh`, one bucket per clock, oldest cause first):*

| bench (per pass) | clocks | CPI | what it models |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 substrate (`tb_ap040_pipe_prog.v`, separate fetch port, 1-clock answers) | **1,426** | **1.68** | the pipeline alone: split I/D, ideal memory |
| bus mode, same bench, one shared port, back-to-back acks, no cache | 4,388 | 5.18 | the shared-port protocol with a zero-latency memory |
| compat wrapper (MMU + reference cache, 16-bit adapter), all hits, MMU off | 4,462 | 5.27 | the core + cache as on the board, without the SoC |
| same, **MMU translation ON** (identity page tables, no TTR) | 5,741 | 6.78 | as above under 68040.library / MuForce page tables |
| sim/ddr3_cpu (real TG68K.vhd crossing, ap040_ram_seq, sdram_ctrl, DDR3 island), code+data+stack in DDR3 fast RAM, IE+DE | 4,672 | 5.52 | the SoC |
| sim/ddr3_cpu, IE only (D-cache off) | 4,919 | 5.81 | |
| reference AP68040, its own program bench (the compat bench's parent) | 6,317 | 7.46 | |

The SoC bench predicts 0.44x with the MMU off; with translation on it scales to ~6,000 clk_38 = **0.35x**, which is the board's 0.33x.  So the hypothesis to check on the board is that SysInfo ran with the MMU translating (68040.library / SetPatch / MuForce): each port transfer then takes one more clock (`ap040_mmu.v` `lk_fresh`: the ATC row must be read for the held address before `pass_ok`).  **Not proven -- a one-line question for Paul: was 68040.library or MuForce running?**

The breakdown (compat bench, per pass of 4,462 clocks, MMU off): retire 19 %; data-read wait 18 %; EA-fetch-to-EX transit 14 %; EA-fetch other (multi-step, serialisation, held redirect) 12 %; EA-calc/ID 9 %; queue-only 7 %; nothing in the pipeline with a fetch on the port 7 %; IF idle 4 %; store held in WB 6 %; frozen by the store's external write 2.4 % (6.9 % in the SoC); EX busy (MUL/DIV) 0.9 %.  Redirect-to-retire (overlay) 12 %.  **Fetch waits** (the four front-end buckets) ~27 %, **memory/crossing** (read wait + store hold + frozen) ~27 %, **branch/refill** 12 % (overlay), **interlocks** (EA-fetch other + EX) ~13 %, retire 19 %.

**The root cause is one number: the shared memory port.**  Per pass there are **1,083 port transfers** (853 fetches, 175 reads, 55 stores) for 847 instructions, and every transfer costs **4 clocks even on a cache hit**: the BCU registers the request (1), the cache accepts it in C_IDLE and looks up in C_LOOK (1), registers `ack_r` (1), the BCU sees the acknowledge and drops the request, and the next request can only go out after a request-low clock (1).  The port is busy 98.4 % of enabled clocks (request up 2.96 clocks per transfer + the ack-clock gap + 274 idle clocks in 17,412).  Fetch alone needs 853 x 4 = 3,412 of the 4,462 clocks.  Everything else in the table is the pipeline waiting for that port.

**What it is NOT.**  Not the clk_38 <-> clk_114 crossing: the SoC adds 4.7 % (210 clocks a pass) over the wrapper bench, almost all of it the store's external write while the core is frozen (`ce_core = clkena_in`: TG68K.vhd's `bus_release` holds the whole core, MMU and cache while an m_* access is outstanding, so posted stores buy nothing on the board).  Not the 16-bit adapter (all hits, no fills).  Not the data cache (IE only costs +5 %).  Not the IF-denied conflict M11 measured: IF is never *denied* here; it is starved by the 4-clock turnaround.

**What-ifs (scratch copies, not in the tree)** on the compat bench: acknowledging a hit combinationally in C_LOOK -> 13,890 clocks for four passes (**1.29x**, but it re-creates the tag-compare -> core-ack path the reference removed for timing); plus back-to-back transfers in the BCU -> 13,475 (1.32x): the handshake tweaks run out quickly because IF can have only one fetch outstanding and must see the answer before issuing the next.  **The 3.1x between the L1 bench and the shared port is what a separate instruction path is worth**; with it the loop would be pipeline-bound near CPI 1.7, i.e. ~1,430 clocks = ~1.4x an A4000/040 on this loop at 37.8 MHz before external store costs.

**Consequence (Paul's ruling the same day, Q21): M14 is IN the plan and comes before any memory-bus / acknowledge-path timing work.**

### M12 — System bring-up: Amiga (size 3, hardware-heavy, Paul's time)
**Goal.** The section 6.1-6.3 Amiga gates pass with the pipelined core selected.
**Entry.** M10 (M9 if FPU deferred), hardware gates 1-3 passed.
**Files.** None planned; fixes go back to the milestone that owns the feature, with a new test first.
**Tests.** Section 6.1-6.3 lists; every failure is reproduced in sim before it is fixed (ILA capture → trace harness program); turbo off first.
**Exit.** Each line of 6.1-6.3 marked pass with the image name in the log.

### M13 — System bring-up: NetBSD/amiga (size 3+, open-ended) -- **DROPPED FOR NOW by Paul 2026-09-24, focus AmigaOS** (the milestone text below is kept as written; nothing in it is scheduled)
**Goal.** 040 kernel boots to multi-user.
**Entry.** M12's Kickstart and MMU-tools lines pass.
**Spec.** NetBSD/amiga `sys/arch/m68k/m68k/trap.c` and `sys/arch/amiga/amiga/locore.s` (how it completes WB1-3 from a $7 frame — this is where the M6 commit-point decision is tested for real), `pmap.c` for the TTR/ATC use.
**Tests.** Stages as pass/fail lines: loadbsd/bootloader loads the kernel; kernel switches the MMU on (first `pmap_bootstrap` completes); reaches the root filesystem prompt (single user); multi-user login. Each stage failing → ILA/serial capture → a sim reproduction added to M6/M7/M9's suites.
**Exit.** Four stages pass, recorded with kernel version and image name.

### M14 — Split instruction and data access paths (IN THE PLAN since 2026-09-24, Paul's ruling Q21) (size 3)
**Entry.** Opened by Paul on 2026-09-24 ("this is important; debugging timing in the memory bus is less useful if we change major CPU aspects later"), on the M11.2 measurement.  **Order: M14 comes BEFORE any memory-bus or acknowledge-path timing work** -- Q12's early acknowledge, the clk_114 -> clk_38 acknowledge family, sdram/crossing tuning, and freeing the core's clock enable during m_* transactions are all parked behind it and get re-measured after it.  The original entry text (IF starved > 15 % *and* decision 2 re-opened) is satisfied by both halves.

**What the measurement says the caches must do (M11.2).**  Size and shape are already right: `ap040_cache.v` IS a real 68040 arrangement -- 4 KB I + 4 KB D, 64 sets x 4 ways x 16-byte lines, physically tagged, tags and data in block RAM, valid bits in the tag row.  What is wrong is the ACCESS: both banks sit behind ONE request port, one request at a time, 4 clocks per hit (register, C_LOOK, registered ack, request-low clock).  The L1 bench shows the pipeline itself needs CPI ~1.7 on SysInfo's loop, which requires IF to receive a longword EVERY clock on a hit while EA-fetch uses the data side.  So M14 is not "instantiate two ap040_cache": two copies of the same 4-clock handshake would buy ~1.27x (fetch and data overlapped, each still at 4 clocks).  It is a **dedicated, pipelined instruction read path**: IF's request addresses the I-bank tag and data arrays at the clock edge it issues, the hit is decided in the next clock (tag compare on the block-RAM outputs), and IF may issue again in that same clock -- exactly the L1 substrate's contract ("answered the next clock").  A hit never reaches the BCU, the MMU's request port, the cache FSM or m_*, so it never touches the clk_38 <-> clk_114 crossing.

**Design (decided here; the alternative is recorded).**
1. **I-side read port on the cache** (`ap040_cache.v`, parameter `IFP`): a read-only copy of the I bank -- tags (64 rows x 4 ways x 22 bits) and data (4 ways x 256 longwords) -- written by exactly the writes the main arrays take for I-bank rows (port-A tag writes, fill and fill-channel data writes), with the I bank's valid bits in flip-flops mirrored from port-A row writes and cleared by port-B row invalidates (fill error, cache-inhibited hit).  The way a fill is overwriting is masked for the whole fill (C_FILL..C_TAGW): the victim's data words change before its tag does.  The main cache keeps every fill, invalidate, CINV sweep, snoop and store rule unchanged -- they are the verified part -- and the shadow can only ever answer "hit" for a line the main arrays also hold.  Block RAM: ~4 x RAMB18 for data + the tag copy; the routed image has **75.5 of 135 BRAM tiles free**, so the display retimer's line buffers (Paul's offer, 2026-09-24) are NOT needed and are not touched.
2. **The core** (`ap040_pipe_core.v`, parameter `IFP`): IF's fetch goes to a small unit in front of the BCU.  IF's request is always granted; the next clock either the wrapper's `ifp_hit` answers it (with the longword), or it becomes a miss and is sent to the BCU's existing fetch slot, answered exactly as today (fills the I bank through the main FSM).  IF keeps its one-outstanding-fetch rule, its drop-after-redirect and its poisoned-word handling: a fast answer is never an error; anything that could fault goes the slow way.
3. **The wrapper** (`ap040_pipe_tg68k_compat.v`): decides whether a fast answer is allowed for the launched address -- CACR.IE, the instruction-cacheable window (the same formula as the slow path, including "instruction fetches from chip RAM bypass"), and translation: **step 1** TC.E = 0 or an ITT0/ITT1 transparent hit with a cacheable CM (physical = logical); **step 2** an I-side ATC read port (a read-only copy of the ATC's I-bank rows, the same mirroring rule, `atc_v` shared), so that an ATC hit with S/permission satisfied answers fast too -- the board runs with the MMU translating (M11.2).  Anything else (ATC miss, walk, fault, cache-inhibited page) goes to the slow path unchanged.
4. **Step 3, the data side**: the same idea for EA-fetch's reads -- a read of the D bank answered the clock after it is issued when no older store is in flight (the BCU's own ordering rule) -- measured after steps 1-2 decide how much is left.
**Rejected**: two full `ap040_cache` + two full `ap040_mmu` instances (assessment 8.2.1 (b)/(c)): duplicates the snoop/CINV/posted-store/walker machinery and still answers in 4 clocks; and widening IF's fetch to a line per transfer through the shared port (halves fetch transfers, keeps the 4-clock turnaround and the conflict).
**Tests.** New `tb/perf/` benches report cycles; correctness is the suite plus new programs with named mutants: an I-cache coherency program (code rewritten in memory: stale without CINV/CPUSHA as on a 68040, fresh after CPUSHA; the fill-in-progress victim; CACR.IE toggled; a cache-inhibited ITT; chip-RAM fetches bypass), the SMC refetch (D10) still refetching, MOVE16, and the DMA-snoop legs.  Mutants: shadow not written on fill (stale code), victim not masked during a fill, IE ignored, ITT CM ignored, chip window cached.
**Exit.** Suite green; the new programs green and each mutant caught; Kickstart legs with an identical ARCHITECTURAL retire trace (cycles will change: the delta is the result); SysInfo loop cycles on the compat and SoC benches; LUT/BRAM measured in the routed whole design with the FPU on and off, and the budget question put to Paul if the FPU image no longer fits under 44,000; the FPU-off configuration builds and passes; a board image and checklist, pending board.

## 5. Verification programme per pillar (what "done" means at the end)

1. Reference suite: every leg of `lib/AP68040/tb/run_tests.sh` passes against the pipelined compat module (M0-M10), with parity on the X and W streams for the program legs.
2. Cputester: the full corpus on the board with the pipelined core equals the reference baseline from M0.3 (3,776 expected; the same 25 generator defects failing). Any slice that passes on the reference and fails on the pipelined core is a bug; any slice that fails on both is compared against the manual and, if the manual agrees with the corpus, is a bug in both (report it to apolkosnik through Paul).
3. Cycle checker: no row over rule without a recorded reason.
4. Mutant log: one caught mutant per test file.
5. System gates: section 6.

## 6. System-level gates

### 6.1 Kickstart and libraries (hardware, Paul)
- DiagROM V2.0 memory test and CPU report (the reference reports "68LC40 FPU: NONE" without FPU).
- Kickstart 3.1 (A1200 40.68) and 3.2 to Workbench; SetPatch; 68040.library installs (needs MMU, gate 2) and reports FPU (gate 3).
- `ENVARC:MMU-Configuration` handling by 68040.library (Trolls AGA case in `whdload-notes.md` as the regression).

### 6.2 CPU/FPU/MMU test programs (hardware, Paul)
- cputester full corpus (M0.2/M0.3 image), all suites.
- Enforcer, MuForce: load, detect a deliberate hit, report the right PC and address.
- VMM: virtual memory enabled and a program paged out and back.
- WHDLoad: the MMU-using titles from `whdload-notes.md` (Trolls AGA with `NOEXPCHIP`) plus five titles that use `QUITKEY`/`NOCACHE` settings chosen from the existing notes.
- FPU: SysInfo/AIBB FPU section, a Mandelbrot demo using the FPU, `t_fpu.s` compiled into an Amiga executable (the cputester FPACK suite is the real oracle).

### 6.3 Games and demos (hardware, Paul; turbo off before blaming the core)
From `findings/aga-chipset/2026-09-19-demo-fix-port.md` (all previously passing on the reference core with the chipset fixes): Sanity "Roots 2.0"; Essence "Crazy Sexy Cool"; RAMJAM "Copperslave" (turbo off); Contraz "Domination"; Desire "Hamazing"; Andromeda "Nexus 7"; Risky Woods; Hybris; Crystal Kingdom Dizzy; WhichAmiga 1.4. Add: three self-modifying-code demos (any RSI/Spaceballs 040 demo that patches its inner loop, plus Team17 "Alien Breed 3D II" which patches code at runtime) and two timing-sensitive titles (Pinball Dreams, Turrican II) to check that the faster CPU with turbo off still keeps chipset timing. Pass = runs as on the reference core; any difference is a regression to reproduce in sim.

### 6.4 NetBSD (hardware, Paul; M13)
Bootloader loads; MMU on; single user; multi user. Kernel version recorded.

## 7. When stuck

1. **Any core (reference or pipelined) vs UAE/cputester disagree — Paul's rule**: the Motorola documentation has the final word: M68040 User's Manual (`doc/MC68040RC25A.PDF`) and the M68000PM Programmer's Reference Manual. The executing agent decides by the manual, cites section and page in the commit message or the milestone log, and records the case in the divergences table below (7.1). It asks Paul **only** when the manual is silent or ambiguous on the point. Reference source comments that cite WinUAE or real hardware for a deviation (`ap040_core.v` names its oracle in many places) are evidence to put in the table, not a verdict; the 25 documented cputester generator defects in `CPUTEST_UPSTREAM_REPORT.md` are already-decided rows.
2. **Reference vs manual disagree** (no UAE evidence either way): same rule — the manual decides, cite it, record the row. The pipelined core then deliberately differs from the reference on that point and the trace comparison for that case is marked as an expected divergence in `compare_trace.py`'s ignore list with the row number.
3. **Reference vs pipelined disagree and the manual covers the point**: rule 1. **If the manual is silent**: the pipelined core follows the reference (parity is the goal until the manual says otherwise) and the case is listed for Paul.
3a. **Manual silent or unclear — look it up online BEFORE asking Paul** (Paul, 2026-09-21). Sources, in this order of weight: other Motorola/Freescale documents (68040 errata, M68040UM addenda, the MC68060 UM for shared behaviour, FPSP/040 release notes); NetBSD m68k sources (`sys/arch/m68k`, the 040 FPSP, `trap.c`, the fault handlers); WinUAE sources (`newcpu.c`, `cpummu.c`) and Toni Wilen's EAB forum threads on real-040 behaviour; MAME m68k and Musashi only as supporting evidence. Record each source (URL + what it says) in the divergences-table row. Manual text still outranks everything; online sources only fill gaps. Ask Paul only if the online sources also conflict or say nothing. Never send project source or the API key anywhere; searching and fetching public pages is fine (WebSearch/WebFetch).

### 7.1 Divergences table

One row per decision taken under rules 1-3. Append; never delete. The `verdict` column is what the pipelined core does, with the manual citation that decides it.

| # | instruction / event | reference core does | UAE / cputester does | manual says (section, page) | verdict | milestone / commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | (example row, delete when the first real one lands) FADD.L with brief index past bit 15 | sign-extends then scales (PRM rule) | corpus plants the operand at the 16-bit-scaled address (generator defect, `CPUTEST_UPSTREAM_REPORT.md` issue 1a) | PRM 2.2 brief extension word: Xn sign-extended to 32 bits, then scaled | reference behaviour; corpus slice expected to fail | M0 (pre-decided) |
| D2 | JMP/JSR to an odd address: stacked PC | JMP: PC+2, PC+6 for (d8,An,Xn)/(d8,PC,Xn); JSR: PC = the odd target, no push (`ap040_core.v` S_JMP1/S_JSR1, validated on the v24 AE group) | WinUAE `gencpu.cpp` i_JMP: `incpc(2)` then `exception3_read_prefetch_only` (PC measured from where the PC had reached); i_JSR at cpu_level > 1 faults on the fetch at the target | M68040UM 8.2.2 p. 8-8: "the address of the instruction that caused the address error" -- does not say which address that is when the fault is the prefetch at the target | follow reference/UAE (manual ambiguous, the online source -- WinUAE, local copy ~/work/amiga/WinUAE/gencpu.cpp -- is specific and agrees with the reference); not asked | M1 c73d43d |
| D3 | Bcc (taken or not) / BSR / DBcc to an odd target | format $2, PC = the branch, address = target & ~1; BSR before its push; DBcc before the condition (`finish_bcc`, S_BCC_EXT, S_DBCC1) | same (gencpu cpu_level >= 4 emits the odd test ahead of cctrue) | M68040UM 8.2.2 p. 8-8: "This includes the case of a conditional branch instruction with an odd branch offset that is not taken" | manual + reference agree | M1 c73d43d |
| D4 | RTS to an odd address; RTE to an odd PC | RTS: pop backed out, SP unchanged, PC = the RTS; RTE: SR and SP restored first, frame carries the restored SR, PC = the RTE (S_RET2, S_RTE_FIN2) | gencpu cpu_level >= 4 rolls A7 back | 8.2.2 silent on the SP; 8.4 silent | follow reference (manual silent, UAE agrees) | M1 c73d43d |
| D5 | MOVE An,(An)+ / MOVE An,-(An): the value stored | the original An (source read before the destination EA) | gencpu: source operand read before the destination EA update | PRM MOVE (4-116) silent | follow reference (manual silent, UAE agrees); tested in postinc.s | M1 c73d43d |
| D6 | CAS / CAS2 when a compare fails: the bus write that ends the locked sequence | no write (S_CAS4: Dc loaded; S_CAS2_F: both Dc loaded) | WinUAE `gencpu.cpp` i_CAS: `if (cpu_level >= 4) { // apparently 68040/060 needs to always write at the end of RMW cycle` -- writes the destination back; i_CAS2: no write-back at any level (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tonioni/WinUAE/master/gencpu.cpp, fetched 2026-09-22) | M68040UM 7.4.5 (Read-Modify-Write Transfers) p. 7-26: "If an operand does not match ... the M68040 still executes a single write transfer to terminate the locked sequence with LOCKE asserted. For the CAS instruction, the value read from memory is written back; for the CAS2 instruction, the second operand read is written back." PRM 4-68 note agrees for CAS (says "destination 1" for CAS2) | follow M68040UM: CAS writes the value read back to <ea>; CAS2 writes the second operand back to (Rn2). Marked `st_rb` in the micro-op; the trace bench writes it as an `L` line and compare_trace.py counts it apart; prog:cas checks each write-back address and that it rewrites the bytes memory holds. The plan's M3 mutant "CAS writes on mismatch" is inverted accordingly ("no write-back" and "writes Du" both caught). Visible later as an access fault on a write-protected page even when the compare fails (M7) | M3 72a5b23 |
| D7 | CAS2 with Dc1 = Dc2 when a compare fails: which memory operand ends in the register | operand 2 (S_CAS2_F writes Dc1, then S_CAS2_F2 writes Dc2) | WinUAE `gencpu.cpp` i_CAS2 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tonioni/WinUAE/master/gencpu.cpp, fetched 2026-09-22): `if (cpu_level >= 4) { // 68040: register update order swapped` -- Dc1 then Dc2, so operand 2 wins on the 68040 (operand 1 on 020/030); the cpu_level-specific code is cputest-validated against real hardware | PRM 4-68 (Dc1, Dc2 fields): "If Dc1 and Dc2 specify the same data register and the comparison fails, memory operand 1 is stored in the data register." -- family text (020/030/040); M68040UM silent | operand 2 -- the WinUAE 68040 rule. Paul's decision 2026-09-22 (Q7): a source verified against real 68040 hardware for a specific case outranks the family-wide PRM text for that case, which is read as the 020/030 behaviour; the M68040UM's own text still outranks everything. Core parameter CAS2_DC_ORDER_020 (0 = 040, tied to 0 everywhere; 1 = 020/030, PRM). cas.s checks it; red with the parameter at 1 | M3 72a5b23; operand 2 since c9ad3a4 |
| D8 | MOVEM <list>,-(An) with An in the list: the value stored for An | initial value minus the size ($703C for A6 = $7040, movem.s) | WinUAE agrees (cputest MVMLE passes on the pipelined core) | PRM MOVEM 4-128: "For the MC68020, MC68030, MC68040, and CPU32, if the addressing register is also moved to memory, the value written is the initial register value decremented by the size of the operation. The MC68000 and MC68010 write the initial register value (not decremented)." | follow the PRM (the plan's M4 text said "stores the initial value" -- that is the 68000/010 rule); the plan mutant is inverted ("stores the initial value" is caught) | M4 dfe2c05 |
| D9 | MOVE16: (Ax)+,(Ay)+ with Ax = Ay; the order of the four longwords | Ax + 16 once; linear order | WinUAE `gencpu.cpp` i_MOVE16: `if (srcreg != dstreg) m68k_areg(regs, srcreg) += 16; m68k_areg(regs, dstreg) += 16;` (once), linear order v[0..3] | PRM MOVE16 4-125: silent on Ax = Ay; "burst reads and writes, which begin with the long word pointed to by the effective address" | +16 once (manual silent, UAE and the reference agree); longwords in address order -- the burst's start-at-EA order is a bus detail (W stream is per address), to be revisited with the M5 bus adapter | M4 99ac8dc |
| D10 | a store into code the pipeline already fetched (self-modifying code without CPUSH) | snoops its fetch queue and refetches (t_integer.s "store into the fetch queue": "the previous fetch-buffered core booted DiagROM and not AmigaOS on hardware with exactly this hazard") | n/a | M68040UM 4.x cache coherency, p. 4-10: "To fully support self-modifying code in any situation, it is imperative that a CPUSHA instruction be executed before the execution of the first self-modified instruction" | follow the reference: the core compares every store with the code EA-fetch, EA-calc, ID and IF hold and refetches after the storing instruction (a superset of the 68040's behaviour; costs a refill only when code is written). Tested by smc.s and diff_tint_m4 | M4 99ac8dc |
| D11 | MOVEC control registers; BKPT | no CAAR; BKPT -> illegal (vector 4) | cputest MOVEC2 / BKPT pass | M68040UM Appendix D (M68000 family summary, D-1, "Control Registers" row): MC68040 = SFC DFC VBR CACR URP SRP TC DTT0 DTT1 ITT0 ITT1 MMUSR (the plan's M4 text lists CAAR, which is 020/030 only); 7.5.2: BKPT runs a breakpoint acknowledge cycle, then "the processor takes an illegal instruction exception" | follow the manual: CAAR ($802) is illegal; BKPT takes vector 4 without the acknowledge cycle (no bus yet; M5's adapter can add the TT=3 cycle) | M4 d51ccb8 |
| D12 | a floating-point instruction (FSAVE/FRESTORE/any cpid 1) on the LC040 build (no FPU) | lib/AP68040 with AP040_HAS_FPU=0 still executes FSAVE/FRESTORE (the ir[11:8]==3 decode is not gated by HAS_FPU): Kickstart 3.1.4's `fsave -(a7)` probe at $F80DA4 sees a NULL frame and sets AttnFlags FPU40 (a reference bug, reported, not changed) | WinUAE fpp.cpp: FSAVE -> fault_if_no_fpu -> fpu_op_illg -> fp_unimp_instruction_exception_pending for a 68040 with fpu_model 0 | M68040UM Appendix A.5/A.5.1 (MC68LC040): every floating-point instruction is unimplemented -> vector 11 with the 8-word format $4 frame (SR, PC of the NEXT instruction, the calculated EA, PC of the faulted instruction) | the pipelined core takes vector 11 (right, Kickstart finds no FPU) but with a format $0 frame and the faulting PC (WRONG: needs format $4, found by the Kickstart sim) -- to be fixed with the FP decode in M10 (the frame needs each FP instruction's length and EA); until then an LC040 FPU-emulation handler reading the frame would misbehave | M5 Kickstart sim; fix M10 |
| D13 | access errors: restart or continuation | pure restart for every fault (CP/CU/CT/CM always 0, WB1S-WB3S always 0, stacked PC = the faulting instruction); posted-store drain errors are fatal (post_err); MOVEM restarts from its first register (loaded registers written again; a control-mode base register written only with the last register) | WinUAE (68040 MMU mode) builds WB slots | M68040UM 8.4.6.3 (p. 8-26..8-29): write-back status for up to three pending writes; case 3 "Normal Write Physical Bus Error ... the WB1S field indicates that it is valid. The FA and WB1A are equivalent"; 8.4.6.7: continuation bits clear -> "resumes normal instruction execution" (a restart) | reads and instruction fetches: the reference's restart, frame words bit for bit (EA = FA, SSW, WB3A = FA, WB3D = the port's last write data). Writes: the manual (Q3, Paul: follow the manual, test against NetBSD and 68040.library) -- stores are synchronous at the core (STORE_POST = 0: WB holds a store until memory has it), so a write fault is precise: on the instruction's last micro-op WB1 is valid, the stacked PC is the next instruction and the handler completes the write (NetBSD trap.c does; it must not be restarted, RMW would double); on an earlier micro-op (MOVEM/MOVE16/a bitfield's or CAS2's first store) the reference's restart. MOVEM: restart (the plan's mutant "MOVEM restarts from the list start" is the intended behaviour). SUPERSEDED for MOVEM by D15 (M7): a MOVEM fault sets CM | M6 667c117, 8250df9 |
| D14 | memory bitfield operands: which bytes are read and written | touches only the field's bytes (lib/AP68040 passes its t_bitfield_mmu.s; checked 2026-09-22) -- the pipelined core's M3 implementation read a longword (+ the fifth byte) whatever the span and faulted on the next page (t_bitfield_mmu.s test 2) | WinUAE `newcpu_common.cpp` x_get_bitfield / x_put_bitfield (local tree /home/paul/work/amiga/WinUAE, the cputest-validated source): B, W, W then B at +2, L, L then B at +4, by (offset mod 8 + width + 7) / 8 | M68040UM silent (bit field operands, 1-32 bits, no access pattern given) | follow WinUAE and the reference (Q7 principle: hardware-validated source for a case the manual leaves open; OPENSTEP 4.2's BFTST at a page end runs on real 68040s): reads and writes touch only the field's bytes, the word/longword before the trailing byte (the order: t_mmu_pipe.s 26-27). A fix of the pipelined core rather than a divergence; recorded for the source trail | M7 a9ecd20, 07134cd |
| D15 | an access error on a MOVEM data access | no CM, EA = FA, restart from the first register (lib/AP68040; its t_mmu.s h_aerr checks EA = FA) | WinUAE 68040 MMU mode sets CM and stacks the MOVEM's EA (`cpummu.cpp` mmu040_movem / mmu040_movem_ea; the RTE side m68k_do_rte_mmu040 restores it); apolkosnik/AP68040 main implements CM (its t_movem_restart.s) | M68040UM 8.4.6.2 p. 8-25 (CM: "set if a data access encounters a bus error for a MOVEM ... the effective address field contains the calculated effective address"; restored on RTE only for mode 6 and PC relative) and 8.4.6.7 p. 8-27 (RTE with CM: EA restored, MOVEM restarted after the EA calculation, all its accesses repeated) | follow the manual: CM set on every MOVEM data fault (read or write, any micro-op; never a pending WB1), EA = the calculated EA, stacked PC = the MOVEM; RTE $7 reads EA and SSW and hands the EA to the MOVEM at the return PC (mode 6 / PC relative only); a fetch fault on that instruction stacks CM and the EA again; -(An) stores send the An update on a trailing micro-op (bus builds) so a restart sees the original An. t_mmu.s via mk_tmmu.py skips its EA = FA check when CM is set. This supersedes D13's MOVEM clause | M7 07134cd |
| D16 | the reference programs' access-error handlers vs the pipelined write model (D13) | t_mmu.s h_aerr: WB3S clear, WB3A = FA, WB3D = the data, no write by the handler (restart model) | NetBSD trap.c writeback() completes WB1-3 | M68040UM 8.4.6.3 case 3: the handler must complete pending write-backs | the programs are used through mk_tmmu.py: h_aerr completes a valid WB1 (byte/word/long/line), skips the WB3A check when WB1 is valid, and takes WB1D as "the write data"; every numbered check stays. Not a core divergence: a test-harness adaptation, recorded because the reference program as written cannot pass on a manual-conforming core | M7 a9ecd20 |
| D17 | when an interrupt is recognised, relative to the next instruction's operand read | sequential: the next instruction has not started, so nothing has been read | WinUAE: interrupts are checked between instructions; no operand of the next instruction has been fetched | M68040UM 8.2.9 p. 8-12..8-14 (a request above the mask becomes *pending* and is taken at an instruction boundary) and 8.3 p. 8-19 (priorities); the manual does not discuss an operand read already issued for the instruction the interrupt is taken in front of, because on a sequential core there is none | the pipelined core defers the interrupt by one instruction boundary when EA-calc's early read for that instruction has already gone out (`i_rd` in ap040_ea_fetch.v), so the instruction runs and the interrupt is taken behind it.  Reason: restarting the instruction after the RTE would read the operand a SECOND time, and an Amiga read-to-clear register (a CIA's ICR at $BFED01, DSKBYTR, INTREQR-style flags) would lose the bits the first read cleared -- behaviour no sequential 68040 can show.  The early read is also held back while a request is pending, so the deferral is one instruction.  t_irq_pipe.s checks 32-33 (a swept level-2 request against a run of reads of a counting register: the bus must see exactly one read per instruction executed) | M9 subset 2026-09-22 |
| D18 | an F-line instruction on the LC040 build (no FPU): which frame, and what goes in its effective-address field | today (and lib/AP68040): vector 11 with a format $0 frame and the instruction's own PC, for every $Fxxx opcode alike (D12 is this bug) | WinUAE, read 2026-09-23 via a Fable agent (local tree /home/paul/work/amiga/WinUAE): a **well-formed** cpid-1 instruction goes `fault_if_no_fpu` (fpp.cpp ~1108) -> `fpu_op_illg` (~1077: `cpu_model == 68040 && fpu_model == 0` -> `fp_unimp_ins`) -> `Exception_build_stack_frame_common` (newcpu_common.cpp ~1638-1643) -> **format $4**, with SR / next PC / $4000\|$2C / `regs.fp_ea` / `regs.instruction_pc`.  A **malformed** one does not: the reserved opclass (extension bits 15:13 = 001) falls to `fpu_noinst` (fpp.cpp ~3607) and a nonexistent opmode is rejected by `fault_if_nonexisting_opmode` (~3535) **before** the FPU check (its comment: "exit immediately and generate normal frame 0 exception 11"), so both take vector 11 with **format $0**; opmodes $78-$7F take **vector 4** (~1236).  The EA field is **0** whenever there is no memory operand -- register to register, FMOVECR, FBcc/FNOP, FTRAPcc, FScc Dn, and an immediate source (the `case 4: // #imm` branch never assigns `ad`); it is NOT the address of the immediate data.  FBcc's next PC is the instruction after the FBcc, taken or not, because the fault precedes the condition (gencpu.cpp ~9296). | M68040UM Appendix A.5.2, Table 12-1 (p. A-6/A-7): the MC68LC040 uses the eight-word format $4 frame for "unimplemented floating-point instructions"; the MC68040 neither generates nor recognises it.  The manual is **silent** on (a) whether a malformed cpid-1 encoding counts as a floating-point instruction and (b) what the EA field holds when there is no memory operand -- it only says "the address of the faulted instruction operand". | Follow the manual where it speaks (well-formed cpid-1 -> vector 11, format $4, next PC, EA, faulted PC) and WinUAE where it does not (malformed cpid-1 and non-cpid-1 F-line -> format $0; opmode $78-$7F -> vector 4; EA = 0 when there is no memory operand; FBcc's next PC never the target).  **Evidence weight, recorded deliberately**: this is NOT a rule-2 hardware-validated case.  WinUAE's cputester never generates FP tests without an FPU (`cputest.cpp` ~4949 `if (isfpp(...) && !currprefs.fpu_model) return;`, and `cputestgen.ini` line 15 says only FPU instruction tests are allowed when an FPU is enabled), so the LC040 no-FPU path is Toni Wilen's reading of the manual plus the shared 68060-FPU-disabled code, i.e. rule-4 evidence.  The one part with rule-2 backing is the plain-illegal F-line sweep (i_ILLG), which confirms format $0 there.  Open, to revisit if a source appears: whether real LC040 silicon really validates the encoding before the FPU-presence check, and what it stacks in EA for register and immediate operands. | M10.0 (in progress) |
| D19 | a floating-point instruction the FPU does not implement (`unimp`, the FPSP route), and an unsupported data type (`unsupp`) | lib/AP68040 `go_fp_unimp` (ap040_core.v, the task): vector 11, **format $2**, the stacked PC is the NEXT instruction's (`pc`), and the address field is the operand's EA when it has one, else the faulting instruction's own PC (`fp_ea_v ? t_a : pc_i`).  `go_fp_unsupp`: vector 55, **format $3**, the next PC for source faults as well as stores, and the EA, or zero when there is no addressable operand. | WinUAE `fpp.cpp` raises both through its post-instruction path (its own comment: "simplification: always mid/post-instruction exception"), and `newcpu_common.cpp` stacks `currpc` with format $3 for the datatype fault.  The reference's own comment records the evidence: the **v24 cputest corpus checks the stacked PC field** and rejects the older "the faulting instruction's own PC" variant that the v20 corpus masked out, and hardware `FABS.P #imm` and `FDMOVE.S Dn` both expect the frame `00,00,42,05,00,00,30,dc,00,00,00,00`. | M68040UM Section 9: vector 11 for unimplemented floating-point instructions, vector 55 for unsupported data types; 8.4.4 format $3 (8-23) is the floating-point post-instruction frame and carries an effective address.  The manual does not say which PC is stacked for each, nor what the address field holds when the operand is a register. | Follow the reference, which is the **rule-2** source here -- it is the cputest-validated one for exactly these two frames, and the corpus rounds that decide it are named in its comment.  unimp = 11 / $2 / next PC / EA-or-own-PC; unsupp = 55 / $3 / next PC / EA-or-zero.  Tested by `fpustub.s` cases 5 and 6; the mutants that stack format $0, a zero address field, or vector 11 with format $2 for the datatype fault are each caught (M10.1 mutants 5, 6, 7). | M10.1 |
| D20 | an illegal effective address for `FMOVE(M).L` to or from FPCR/FPSR/FPIAR (opclass 100/101), and which addressing modes are legal at all | lib/AP68040 `fp_crm` (ap040_core.v:3961-4010): Dn only when ONE register is selected, An only when the single register is FPIAR, an immediate only as a SOURCE, a PC-relative DESTINATION rejected, and `(An)+` / `-(An)` accepted in BOTH directions; every rejection is `go_fp_fline` -- vector 11, **format $0**, the instruction's own PC | WinUAE `fpp.cpp` case 5 (local tree /home/paul/work/amiga/WinUAE, read 2026-09-23, lines ~3196-3350): the same four rules, `fpu_noinst` for each, and the memory arm rejects only PC-relative -- it explicitly updates An for BOTH `(An)+` and `-(An)` on a store.  Its 68040 also allows an EMPTY register list on An (only the 68060 F-lines that).  Note the contrast with opclass 110/111 at case 6/7, where WinUAE DOES enforce "(An)+ and PC-relative not supported for stores, -(An) not supported for loads" | M68000PM/AD FMOVEM (control registers), p. 5-91/5-92: "Memory-to-Register -- Only control addressing modes or the postincrement addressing mode can be used", "Register-to-Memory -- Only control alterable addressing modes or the predecrement addressing mode", Dn "Only if a single ... register is selected", An "Only if the floating-point instruction address register is the single register selected".  **But the manual's own addressing-mode TABLE on the memory-to-register page lists `-(An)` (mode 100) as well**, so its prose and its table disagree on exactly the two modes in question.  M68040UM is silent | Follow WinUAE and the reference: Dn single only, An FPIAR only (empty list included), immediate source only, PC-relative destination rejected, `(An)+` and `-(An)` allowed both ways.  Rejections take the plain F-line, format $0, own PC -- which closes part of M10.0's recorded over-generosity, **but only with `HAS_FPU = 1`**: with no FPU nothing has looked at the effective address and WinUAE's LC040 path gives every well-formed cpid-1 opcode the format $4 frame (D18), so that stays.  Evidence weight: the PRM is rule 3 and contradicts itself here; WinUAE's FP code is rule 2 for a core WITH an FPU (its cputester generates FP tests once an FPU is enabled), so it decides.  Tested by `fpureal_cr.s` cases 9-11 | M10.2 |
| D21 | FMOVEM of the FP registers (opclass 110/111): which addressing modes are legal, which register the mask bits name, and the order of the three longwords | lib/AP68040 `fp_mvm` (ap040_core.v:4010-4048) and `S_FPU_MVM` (:4390): Dn, An and immediate are F-line both ways; a STORE rejects `(An)+` and the PC-relative modes, a LOAD rejects `-(An)`; a load always maps mask bit 7 to FP0, a store follows the mode field's convention; a predecrement store consumes the mask from its LSB; and a store whose convention disagrees with its address direction writes each register's three longwords REVERSED.  Its comment names WinUAE `fmovem2mem` as the source | WinUAE `fpp.cpp` case 6/7 (local tree, read 2026-09-23, lines ~3400-3432): `// FMOVEM FPP->Memory: (An)+ and PC relative modes not supported` and `// FMOVEM Memory->FPP: -(An) not supported`, each `fpu_noinst`.  Note the CONTRAST with opclass 100/101 at case 5 (D20), where WinUAE enforces no such thing -- the two instruction groups genuinely differ | M68000PM/AD FMOVEM (data registers) p. 5-85/5-86 is explicit for the three standard forms: a control mode transfers upward, order FP0-FP7; predecrement is register-to-memory ONLY, decrements by 12 before each store, order FP7-FP0, An left pointing at the last image; postincrement is memory-to-register ONLY, upward, FP0-FP7.  It says nothing about an encoding whose mask convention disagrees with its addressing mode, because no assembler emits one.  M68040UM is silent | The three standard forms follow the MANUAL and come out of this implementation unchanged (`fpureal_mvm.s` cases 1 and 2 check the layout and the round trip).  The two conventions-disagree cases follow the reference and WinUAE, which is rule-2 evidence for a core WITH an FPU, and case 3 pins both quirks.  The EA rejections are the manual's "only" clauses read as exclusions, and WinUAE agrees explicitly; each is the ordinary F-line, format $0, own PC, as for D20.  Still out: the DYNAMIC register list, whose mask comes from a data register -- `P_FPU` does not sequence a register read before the transfers, so it keeps M10.0's format $4 frame | M10.4 |
| D22 | a floating-point instruction whose EFFECTIVE ADDRESS the 68040 rejects -- an address register as source or destination, a data register too narrow for the format, an immediate or PC-relative destination | lib/AP68040 rejects each one with `go_fp_fline`: vector 11, **format $0**, the instruction's own PC, and no FPIAR side effect (`ap040_core.v:3926-4067`).  But its FPSP route runs FIRST: an opmode `op_in_hw` does not implement reports as UNIMPLEMENTED whatever the EA is | WinUAE `fpp.cpp` `get_fp_value` runs `fault_if_unimplemented_680x0` BEFORE it rejects a Dn or An source, which is exactly the split.  **`t_fpu.s` pins both halves and names the cputest rounds for them**: tests 76-79 are `fabs.b a0,fp1` and `fabs.d d0,fp1` (cputest FABS.B/0001 and FABS.D/0001) and expect the plain format $0 F-line because FABS is hardware; test 326 is `fint.x a1,fp1` -- the same illegal EA with an FPSP opmode -- and expects vector 11 with **format $2** and the next PC | M68000PM/AD gives the legal addressing modes per instruction, so the REJECTION is the manual's; what the manual does not say is which of the two faults wins when an FPSP opmode has an illegal EA.  M68040UM is silent | Follow the reference and WinUAE, which agree and are the cputest-validated pair for a core WITH an FPU: the EA rejection gives the ordinary F-line (format $0, own PC) **only when the opmode is one the unit implements**; otherwise the unimplemented route wins (vector 11, format $2, next PC, address field = the faulting PC, D19's no-operand shape).  Decode carries a copy of `ap040_fpu.v`'s `op_in_hw` list, copied verbatim so the two cannot disagree.  Still out, and recorded: PACKED into a data register, which is the datatype fault (vector 55) and keeps format $4 for now | M10.6 60d6f0b |
| D23 | a TRACE and an INTERRUPT pending at the SAME instruction boundary | lib/AP68040 takes the INTERRUPT first and then delivers the displaced trace at the interrupt HANDLER's entry (its `texc_pend` machinery, `ap040_core.v` `fetch_next` and `S_EXC_JMP`).  Its own comment calls this **OPEN**: "it may be a spurious trace no real 68040 produces", kept because "the ordering these tests check is the part hardware actually settled".  `t_exceptions.s` tests 142/143 assert it | WinUAE takes the interrupt and **DROPS** the trace: every `Exception()` calls `exception_check_trace()`, which unsets `SPCFLAG_TRACE`/`SPCFLAG_DOTRACE` and clears both T bits, and its `< 68040` guard means the 040 never re-arms the pending trace.  The reference quotes this code in the same comment | **M68040UM 8.3 is explicit and neither of them follows it.**  Table 8-4 puts **Trace in priority group 6 and Interrupt in group 8**, "with 0 as the highest priority", so the trace is processed FIRST.  The same section then says what happens to the loser: "As soon as the M68040 has completed exception processing for a condition when an interrupt exception is pending, it begins exception processing for the interrupt exception instead of executing the exception handler for the original exception condition", with the TRAP example -- "When the processor resumes normal instruction execution, it is in the interrupt handler, which returns to the trap exception handler" | **Follow the MANUAL (authority rule 1), since the two implementations disagree with it AND with each other, and the reference admits its own is open.**  The trace frame is built first; the interrupt, still pending on the pins, is then taken in front of the TRACE HANDLER's first instruction -- which this core already does for every exception (t_exceptions 93-105 pass).  The interrupt handler returns to the trace handler, which returns to the traced program.  **Consequence: `t_exceptions.s` tests 142/143 CANNOT pass on this core and are not meant to** -- they assert the reference's model.  `tb/irq_asm/t_trirq_pipe.s` asserts the manual's, deterministically (T1 and T0 legs), and three mutants hold it.  **What a program would SEE differently, in one sentence, because this is the first place this plan knowingly diverges from BOTH the reference core and UAE: when a trace and an interrupt fall on the same instruction boundary, a debugger on this core gets the TRACE exception first with the interrupt frame stacked on top of it (so the interrupt handler returns into the trace handler, and the trace frame's stacked PC is the traced instruction's successor), where the reference core would run the interrupt handler first and deliver a trace frame whose stacked PC is the INTERRUPT HANDLER's entry, and WinUAE would run the interrupt handler and produce no trace at all** -- so if an Amiga debugger single-stepping through interrupt-heavy code, or NetBSD's trap path, ever misbehaves in a way that looks like a spurious or missing single-step, this is the first divergence to check | M9.T step 4 |
| D24 | a single- or double-precision DENORMAL source (vector 55): what ETEMP holds in the BUSY frame | lib/AP68040 (and upstream main): the raw left-aligned memory image `{r_din[95], r_din[94:80], 16'd0, r_din[63:0]}` -- for a single, the whole operand sits in bits 95:64 and ETEMP's mantissa is zero | WinUAE `fp_unimp_datatype` (local tree, fpp.cpp ~1054): the operand converted by `float32/64_to_floatx80_allowunnormal` -- 0.fraction, UNNORMALIZED -- with ETEMP's exponent word overwritten by $3F80 / $3C00 (and the sign lost with it: `et[0] = 0x3f800000`) | M68040UM silent on ETEMP's contents; the FPSP (`get_op.sa src_sd_dnrm`) rewrites ETEMP_EX with its own bias $3F81 / $3C01, keeps the sign bit from ETEMP_EX, and normalizes the mantissa -- so the mantissa must be 0.fraction | Follow WinUAE's layout, keeping the sign (the FPSP reads it): ETEMP = {sign, $3F80/$3C00, 0.fraction}.  Evidence: the FPSP source (what the consumer requires) plus 68040.library on this core -- 2^-127 and 2^-74 come back exact; the raw image gave a negative unnormal.  The exponent word is WinUAE's and is overwritten by the FPSP anyway | M10.10 fdc835c |
| D25 | a DEFERRED enabled FP exception (a released operation's) when the next FP instruction is FSAVE or FRESTORE | M10.3 took it in front of EVERY FP instruction, FSAVE and FRESTORE included | WinUAE: FSAVE saves the pending state (fsave_data of fpsr_check_arithmetic_exception), `fpuop_restore` never calls fp_exception_pending | M68040UM 9.x: FSAVE does not report pending exceptions (the state frame carries them, E1/E3); FRESTORE installs a state | Follow lib/AP68040 (S_FSAVE1 / S_FREST1-BD) and WinUAE, which agree: the unit prepares the E1/E3 frame when the exception is deferred (pend_capture); an FSAVE that finds it EXTRACTS it (no exception); an FSAVE that finds none takes it (the reference's frameless fallback); FRESTORE never takes it -- NULL/IDLE discard it, an E1/E3 frame re-arms it for the next FP instruction unless the frame resumes the unit.  Upstream `t_fpu_frames.s` tests 30-37 and fpureal_busy case 8 | M10.10 25940fb |
4. **A test needs an RTL change in `lib/AP68040`** (e.g. a debug tap): do not make it; do it in the bench hierarchically, or ask Paul (the submodule is pinned; `tools/test_ap040.sh` explains the shadow-copy rule).
5. **Budget or timing stop rule triggers**: stop, record, ask.
6. **A hardware gate fails**: get the ILA/serial capture from Paul, reproduce in sim (trace harness program or `sim/ddr3_cpu` leg), fix in the owning milestone with a new test first, re-run that milestone's exit checks, then re-request the gate. Never fix on the board.
7. **A `sim/ddr3_cpu` result contradicts the core benches**: read the bench-traps memory first; check the enable cadence and whether the numbers moved; rerun alone.
8. Ask Paul when: any decision in "READ THIS FIRST" item 2 looks wrong; the M6 commit-point/WB1 choice conflicts with what NetBSD or 68040.library expects; the FPU does not fit; cputester data cannot be generated; a demo needs a chipset fix rather than a core fix.

(2) Mode 7 registers 5-7 are reserved for every coprocessor command, so a primary word carrying one is malformed and takes format $0 with its own PC whatever the extension word says ($F27D, $F27E).  (3) FSAVE/FRESTORE with an illegal effective address are F-line BEFORE they are privileged -- the corpus runs $F300/$F301 and $F318-$F31A in USER mode and expects vector 11, not vector 8.  So the order is encoding, then privilege, then the FPU.  Two of the three came from porting the reference core's structure, which is right for a core WITH an FPU; PLAN section 7 rule 2 says the hardware-validated corpus outranks it, and here it did.  The EA-legality matrix M10.0 deliberately deferred is therefore half-closed already, by measurement rather than by reading |

That is the milestone to size when someone asks for an FPU image.  **And one practical consequence of the routed FPU build belongs with that decision: at 67.7 % full with 1,088 LUTs of headroom, an FPU image and the ILA CANNOT COEXIST (the ILA costs ~6,300). ## 9. Open questions for Paul

1. cputester corpus: is apolkosnik's `data040.zip` (DATA_VERSION 24) available, or is there a Windows WinUAE build to run `cputestgen`? Without one of these, M0.2-M0.4 and the board baseline cannot happen.
2. Does the reference's `run_cputest.py` (mentioned in its `doc/tests-README`, not in the public repo) exist in `~/work/fpga/Xilinx/artix7/minimig` or elsewhere? Reusing it saves ~1 week in M0.4.
3. M6 design point (1): the pipelined core will have a real WB stage, so the manual's WB1-3 fields can be filled and NetBSD's `trap.c` would complete the pending write itself. The reference deliberately never marks WB valid (restart model). Which behaviour do you want, given both 68040.library and NetBSD must be happy? The plan assumes the manual's behaviour with a test on both consumers.
4. If the FPU trips the LUT stop rule in M10: is an openaars build without the FPU acceptable for the pipelined core (as `AP040_HAS_FPU=0`) while the integer/MMU work ships?
   **Probably moot, 2026-09-23**: the lifted `ap040_fpu` is 9,767 LUTs out of context (budget 12,000), the core is 17,030 and the MMU 1,167, so the AP040 block comes to about 27,960 -- slightly SMALLER than the reference block's 28,431.  The whole design with the pipelined core and the MMU is 31,298 today, so the FPU should land it near 41,000 against the 44,000 budget.  A routed build with `HAS_FPU=1` is what actually settles it, and that waits on M10's sequencing work.
5. Work branch and location: the pipelined repo is a separate clone today; should it become a second submodule (`lib/AP68040-pipelined`) or a branch of `lib/AP68040`?
6. Section 6.3's added titles (self-modifying and timing-sensitive) are my picks; replace with ones you have on disk.
7. CLOSED 2026-09-22 (Paul): CAS2 with Dc1 = Dc2 and a failed compare follows WinUAE's 68040 rule, operand 2 (D7); CAS2_DC_ORDER_020 = 0.
8. (M5, BLOCKING gate 1) Kickstart needs interrupts: in the Kickstart simulation 3.1.4 runs identically on both cores through intuition's init and then idles in `STOP #$2000` waiting for an interrupt; the pipelined core has no interrupts/STOP/RESET (M9), takes vector 4 and Alerts. So gate 1 as written (Kickstart to Workbench / floppy CLI) cannot pass before M9. Options: (a) pull a minimal M9 subset (IPL sampling with autovectors, STOP, RESET) forward before gate 1, leaving trace/M-bit/throwaway frames in M9; (b) run gate 1 now as a DiagROM-only smoke test and keep the Kickstart gate for after M9; (c) keep the order and hold gate 1 until after M9 (M7/M8/M10 first). The agent's suggestion is (a): it is the only way the board sees the pipelined core before M10.
9. (M7, gate-2 bitstream timing) Whole-design builds with the MMU, pipelined 5c95c97: **ILA off** (`build/stage_ap040_pipe_m7d` in the worktree) meets timing except the long-standing clk_gen_sdram -> clk_114 SDRAM output path (-0.479 ns, 16 endpoints -- the reference builds show the same path at -0.48..-0.55 ns, e.g. stage_ap040_e2t4b); **ILA on** (`stage_ap040_pipe_m7e`) misses by 0.73 ns: TG68K's acknowledge -> WB's store hold -> the stall chain back through EX, EA-fetch, EA-calc and ID -> IF's queue enable (clk_114 -> clk_38, -0.73 ns), and a MOVEM path inside EA-fetch (clk_38, -0.67 ns). Two acknowledge paths were already cut without cost (b210f0e: EX's redirect; 5c95c97: WB's SMC refetch). Options: (a) take the ILA-off image to the board for gate 2 (no CPU ILA; the reference core's ILA builds are what the debug flow uses anyway); (b) cut the store-hold backpressure: synchronous stores release WB a clock after the acknowledge (costs a clock per store) or a one-entry skid register between EX and WB (area, a sequencing change); (c) relax the CPU clock (CPU_CLK_DIVIDE) for the pipelined build. The agent's suggestion: (a) now; (b) with the skid register in M11 if the ILA is needed on the pipelined core.
10. (2026-09-23, board access) **The JTAG cable is held by your Vivado GUI** (pid 3186338, running since 2026-09-21; it owns hw_server on TCP:3121 and two cs_servers). 12. **DEFERRED by Paul 2026-09-24 until the whole plan has completed (see Q23: the store completion is registered instead).**  (2026-09-23, M11.1) **May the wrapper give the core an acknowledge one clk_114 cycle early?** The pipelined core's one structural timing problem is that TG68K registers `x_ack_r` on the clk_114 edge immediately before the core's clk_38 edge, so EA-fetch's redirect decision and everything it fans out to have 8.815 ns (PLAN M11.1 has the derivation and the two failed local fixes). The SDRAM and DDR3 sequencers know a cycle in advance that data will land, so a `will_ack` strobe would double that window at no cost in clocks. It is a change in `rtl/soc/TG68K.vhd` and `rtl/soc/ap040_ram_seq.vhd`, which the REFERENCE core also uses, so I have not touched it. The alternative is entirely inside the pipelined core: register the redirect and pay one clock on every RTS/RTE/JMP (An)/vector fetch. One line: "wrapper" / "pay the clock" / "leave it, M11 will tell us how much it costs".
