16. **(2026-09-23, M10.1) Two things I decided rather than asked, both reversible, both recorded so you can overrule them.**
    (a) **An FP instruction serialises.**  It waits in EA-fetch until EX and WB have drained before the request goes out.  The reason is safety, not throughput: once the unit has the command it has changed architectural state, and a redirect by an older instruction -- a branch resolved in EX, a MOVE to SR, an exception -- would flush the stage that sent it and leave that state behind.  With nothing older in the pipe there is no such redirect.  It does NOT cost the background release: after `accepted` the instruction leaves and integer work runs on, which `tb_ap040_pipe_fpu_bg.v` measures.  What it costs is the drain in front of every FP instruction, which on FP-dense code is real.  The alternative is a flush-aware abort in the unit, which I did not want to design before there is a workload to measure it against.  M11's business.
    (b) **The out-of-context number for `ap040_pipe_core` alone is no longer comparable across M10.1**, and I have stopped quoting it.  The core now has the `fp_*` port group as OUTPUT ports; out of context, synthesis must keep everything that drives an output port, so the measurement includes logic that the real design prunes (the wrapper leaves them unconnected with `AP040_HAS_FPU = 0`).  It went 17,030 -> 17,200 LUTs and -0.574 -> -1.547 ns purely for that reason.  The comparable measurement, which I ran instead, is the **wrapper** out of context with `AP040_HAS_FPU = 0` -- the configuration that ships -- and the routed whole design remains what the stop rule is tested against (Q13).

17. **(2026-09-23, M10 and the FPU image) Making FSAVE work is what turns the remaining FPU gaps from dormant into live -- do you want that before 68040.library is in the picture?**
    Where it stands: with `AP040_HAS_FPU = 1` the core now executes the FP data movement, the arithmetic the unit implements, the control registers, FMOVEM and the enabled arithmetic exceptions.  FSAVE and FRESTORE still take the F-line, so **Kickstart's `fsave -(a7)` probe reports NO FPU**, AmigaOS never emits a floating-point instruction, and every remaining gap (the transcendentals through `unimp`, the packed type through `unsupp`, a store's enabled exception) stays dormant.
    The moment FSAVE answers with a NULL/IDLE frame, the probe reports an FPU and AmigaOS starts using one -- and the first `FSIN` takes vector 11 into an FPSP that is not installed until 68040.library is.  On a bare Kickstart that is an alert, not a wrong answer, but it is a REGRESSION against today's behaviour.
    Options: **(a)** build FSAVE/FRESTORE now and treat "FPU images need 68040.library" as the rule (which is what a real 68040 needs too); **(b)** build it but leave `AP040_HAS_FPU = 0` in every image until the FPSP story is settled, so it is only ever exercised in simulation; **(c)** leave FSAVE F-lining and keep the FPU invisible to the OS while the arithmetic is proven by `t_fpu.s` in sim.
    My recommendation was **(b)**: the gate image and every image you load stay LC040 exactly as now, the FPU work keeps landing and gets tested in simulation, and the first `HAS_FPU = 1` image is a deliberate experiment with 68040.library on the disk rather than a surprise.
    **RULED 2026-09-23 by the COORDINATING SESSION, not by Paul, who can overrule it** (the same standing as Q13 and Q15): **build it, option (b)**.  The reasoning recorded with the ruling: this is simulation-only work whose risk is entirely in what a BITSTREAM does, every image stays `AP040_HAS_FPU = 0` so nothing Paul loads changes, and leaving the instruction F-lining to keep Kickstart's probe answering "no FPU" would be hiding an incomplete feature behind a switch instead of finishing it -- the FPSP question does not go away by postponing it.  Three conditions came with it and are being honoured:
    1. **every gate and board image stays `HAS_FPU = 0`** until Paul explicitly asks for an FPU image -- said here, in STATUS, and in both hardware checklists;
    2. the Kickstart bench gets its **explicit `HAS_FPU = 0` leg BEFORE FSAVE lands**, so the LC040 baseline cannot move underneath the work, and a `HAS_FPU = 1` leg alongside it so what AmigaOS does once the probe answers is visible;
    3. when that `HAS_FPU = 1` leg reaches the first transcendental, **do not build an FPSP**: record exactly what it needs (vector 11 sequencing, frame contents, what 68040.library installs) as a milestone of its own with an estimate, and raise it for Paul.  The first FPU image stays a deliberate experiment with him.
    **Condition 3, answered by measurement 2026-09-23 11:47 and better than expected: it never reached a transcendental.**  With the FPU visible, Kickstart 3.1.4 runs to trackdisk's first track read in 2,289,114 instructions -- the reference core's count to the digit -- taking ZERO F-line and ZERO unimplemented-instruction traps, and ExecBase's AttnFlags comes out **$004F**, so the FPU is detected and used.  The LC040 leg is bit-identical to its own baseline, which is what the explicit `HAS_FPU = 0` leg was added to guarantee.
    So **the FPSP is not a boot problem**; it is a problem for what runs after the boot -- Workbench, 68040.library's own installation, and applications that call the transcendentals.  Nothing was built for it, as the condition asked.  What it would need, for whoever sizes it: vector 11 delivery with the format $2 frame this core already builds (M10.1 D19), FSAVE/FRESTORE of the `$4130` frame so the package can save and resume its state (M10.5's recorded gap -- the 13-longword payload and the variable `-(An)` step), and the package itself, which on a real Amiga comes from 68040.library rather than from the CPU.  A rough size: the `$4130`/`$4160` frames are a week of the same kind of work as M10.4, and the rest is software Paul already has on disk.

18. **(2026-09-23, 10:40) THE STOP RULE HAS TRIGGERED AGAIN, on a HIGH-effort routed build, and I have stopped the FP RTL family.**
    `build/stage_ap040_pipe_m103` (sources aa67f0a, `IMPL_EFFORT=high`, MMU on, **FPU off**, ILA off) has **37 failing endpoints** where m101 had 16.  The breakdown matters, because most of it is not the CPU:
    - 16 endpoints, -0.472: the known `clk_gen_sdram -> clk_114` SDRAM data path.  Present in every reference build.  Not ours.
    - 19 endpoints, -0.035: `clk_ddr100` intra, `ddr3_island/rst_sync_q_reg[3]` -> `u_core/active_row_q_reg[3][11]/R` -- the DDR3 controller's reset fanout.  Not the CPU, and no CPU change can have caused it except by moving placement.
    - 1 endpoint, -0.129: `amiga_clk/clk7_en_reg_reg` -> `sdram/clk7_enD_reg`, the chipset's 7 MHz enable.  Also not the CPU.
    - **1 endpoint, -0.046: `g_mmu.mmu/atc_ram/mem_reg_3/CLKBWRCLK` -> `tg68k/addr_reg[4]/CE`, `clk_38 -> clk_114`.**  This IS a CPU path, and it is the same family Q15 flagged at -0.022 on a default-effort build of cc6c12f -- the island's OUTPUT crossing, which `cpu.xdc` already gives a two-cycle multicycle.
    Everything else meets, and one number got BETTER: `clk_114 -> clk_38` is +0.063 where m101 had +0.005.  Area went DOWN, 31,581 against m101's 31,851, so the `HAS_FPU = 0` path did not grow across the three steps.
    What makes this hard to read is the spread: that one family has measured **+0.620 (m11c), -0.022 (m11b), and now -0.046 (m103)** on nearly the same design.  Options: **(a)** treat it as placement noise and carry on, on the evidence of a second build; **(b)** fix it in RTL -- register the ATC output before it reaches the wrapper's address-register enable, which is inside the MMU the REFERENCE core also instantiates, so it is a Q12-shaped question; **(c)** relax the crossing in `cpu.xdc` from two cycles to three, which is a constraint change on a path the reference core also uses.
    **What I did meanwhile**: started a SECOND high-effort build of the tip (`stage_ap040_pipe_m104`, sources 61b6ed2 -- M10.4 included, so MORE logic, not less) and stopped adding FP RTL.
    **RESOLVED BY THAT BUILD, 2026-09-23 11:14, and the answer is clean.**  `stage_ap040_pipe_m104`: **WNS -0.483, TNS -5.924, 16 failing endpoints, every one the SDRAM path (`dr_d[n]` -> `sdata_reg_reg[n]/D`), and not one CPU endpoint.**  `clk_38 -> clk_114` is **+0.296** where m103 had -0.046; the 19 `clk_ddr100` endpoints and the `dll_28` one are gone; `clk_114 -> clk_38` +0.015; 31,638 LUTs (49.9 %).  So m103's twenty-one extra endpoints were PLACEMENT, on a design with one commit LESS in it.
    **RULED 2026-09-23 by the COORDINATING SESSION, not by Paul, who can overrule it**: **keep the stop rule as written -- a single high-effort routed build -- with the refinement that a repeat on the SAME endpoint family across two consecutive high-effort builds is the real signal, while a single -0.046 that clears on the next build is noise to RECORD, not to act on.  Both numbers keep being logged and the endpoint family keeps being named every time; if that crossing ever fails twice running on the same endpoints, stop and ask rather than rebuilding a third time.**  That family has now measured **+0.620 (m11c), -0.022 (m11b), -0.046 (m103), +0.296 (m104)** -- a 0.67 ns spread with no monotonic relationship to the RTL, which is what "placement noise on a two-cycle crossing" looks like.  **The tip passes the stop rule as written and the FP RTL family is unblocked**; this entry stays as the record, and the standing question underneath it is whether a single -0.046 endpoint on a rebuild should stop the work again or whether the rule should read "two consecutive high-effort builds", which is a judgement for you.  My recommendation: keep the rule as it is (it cost one build to resolve and that build was worth having), and treat a repeated failure on the SAME endpoint as the real signal.

19. **(M9.T step 5, 2026-09-23) The format $7 FAULT ADDRESS for a bus error on an instruction FETCH: does the frame carry the LONGWORD-ALIGNED address of the fetch, or the address of the word that faulted?**  `t_exceptions.s`'s fetch-berr test arms `$E5A` and this core reports **FA = `$E58`** -- the aligned address of the fetch that covered it -- where lib/AP68040 reports `$E5A`.  M68040UM calls FA "the address of the faulted access", and for a longword instruction fetch that is arguably the aligned one, so the manual does not settle it and neither implementation is evidence for the other.  **This is the sort of thing your cputest disks may already answer**: it is the first thing to look for when the AE / ODD_STK groups run on the board (they are the groups that check access-error frames), and a corpus record or a hardware capture would close it outright.  Until then the M-bit section of `t_exceptions.s` stays OUT of the suite extract rather than being adjusted to whichever answer we prefer, and the divergence is recorded here rather than guessed.

20. **(M11, 2026-09-23) Which image do you want, because it decides M14 (the split I/D caches) outright.**  Measured: IF is the lowest-priority user of the shared memory port and is denied while data holds it for **19 %** of all cycles on the loop-heavy benchmark, 15 % on exception-heavy code, 7 % on straight-line integer code.  The plan's gate is 15 %, so **split caches are JUSTIFIED on throughput** -- but the routed FPU build leaves **1,088 LUTs** of the 44,000 budget and they cost far more than that.  So **M14 is buildable for an LC040 image and not for an FPU image**: it is filed against your decision rather than scheduled, and nothing needs doing until you choose.

    **ANSWERED 2026-09-24 by Paul (Q21 below): M14 is IN, ahead of the memory-bus timing work.  The FPU/LUT question it raised is now Q22.**

21. **RULED BY PAUL 2026-09-24: split instruction and data caches (M14) are IN the plan, for performance and for bus timing.**  "This is important; debugging timing in the memory bus is less useful if we change major CPU aspects later."  Consequences recorded in M14: it comes after the merge and the M11.2 measurement and BEFORE any memory-bus or acknowledge-path timing work (Q12's early acknowledge, the clk_114 -> clk_38 acknowledge paths, sdram/crossing tuning), which is parked behind it and re-measured after it; the caches are sized and shaped from M11.2; tag and data arrays in block RAM; if caches and FPU do not both fit, BOTH configurations are built and measured and the choice is Paul's -- the executing agent does not drop the FPU, and the FPU-off LC040 configuration must keep working.  Paul also offered the display retimer's spare line buffers as a BRAM source, to be used ONLY if the caches need block RAM that is not otherwise free: the routed image has 75.5 of 135 BRAM tiles free, so they are not touched.

22. **(OPEN, M14) The LUT budget once the caches are in -- MEASURED 2026-09-24.**  Routed at IMPL_EFFORT=high, no ILA, sources 4fbc558 (= the merge 629eab9): **caches + FPU (`stage_ap040_pipe_m14b_fpu`): 44,231 LUTs (69.8 % of the 63,400 on the part), 71.5 of 135 BRAM tiles, timing met apart from the 16 known SDRAM endpoints (clk_38 +0.494, clk_114 -> clk_38 +0.399, clk_38 -> clk_114 +1.157); caches without FPU (`stage_ap040_pipe_m14b_lc040`): 33,212 LUTs (52.4 %), 71.5 BRAM tiles, the same 16 endpoints only (clk_38 +0.958, clk_114 -> clk_38 +0.310).**  Against the pre-M14 FPU image (`timing_fpu_ctr`, 43,174 LUTs, 59.5 BRAM) M14 costs **+1,057 LUTs and +12 BRAM tiles**.  So both fit the device with room, and **the FPU image is 231 LUTs over this plan's own 44,000 headroom rule** (section 1.3), which is a rule for timing margin, not a device limit -- and the build that exceeds it meets timing.  The FPU was not dropped (Paul's instruction), and the LC040 configuration builds and passes.  **My recommendation: raise the budget to ~48,000 (75 %) and keep both caches and FPU;** the only thing the headroom rule protected -- an ILA beside an FPU image (~6,300 LUTs) -- was already impossible before M14 (Q9), so M14 does not change what can be debugged on an FPU image.  One line: "raise it" / "keep 44,000 and ship the LC040 image with caches".

23. **(OPEN, M14, 2026-09-24) Confirm the registered store completion (`STDONE_REG = 1`), or take Q12 instead.**  The first routed M14 build (`stage_ap040_pipe_m14_fpu`, MMU=1 FPU=1) missed timing by 1.48 ns on 2,432 endpoints, 2,173 of them the clk_114 -> clk_38 acknowledge family of M11.1: `x_ack_r` -> the BCU's `st_done` -> WB's store hold -> EX's stall -> the whole stall chain -> IF's request, which now also addresses the read paths' block RAMs.  That family had run at a few tens of ps of slack in every build; M14's extra block RAM beside the core pushed it over.  I cut it in the core (4fbc558): WB's synchronous store completes the clock AFTER memory acknowledges it, so the acknowledge ends at the BCU's flip-flops.  **Cost, measured: +2.7 % on SysInfo's loop with the MMU off, +0.7 % with it on, +0.7 % on the LC040 Kickstart boot (26,742,676 -> 26,935,272 cycles); the architectural traces are unchanged.**  This is the first option of Q9(b); the alternative that costs no clock is Q12's early acknowledge from the wrapper, which touches TG68K.vhd / ap040_ram_seq.vhd (shared with the reference core) and stays yours.  My recommendation: **keep STDONE_REG = 1 now** (the design is not timing-clean without it or Q12), and take Q12 later as part of the parked bus work if you want the clock back.  One line: "keep it" / "revert it and do Q12".
    **RULED BY PAUL 2026-09-24: "keep it".  STDONE_REG = 1 (4fbc558) stays.  Q12 (the early acknowledge from the wrapper) is DEFERRED until the whole plan has completed -- it is NOT part of the parked bus work that follows M14.**

## 10. Totals

| phase | milestones | person-weeks |
|---|---|---|
| infrastructure | M0 | 2 |
| integer ISA, sizes, EA modes | M1-M4 | 9.5 |
| bus, precise faults | M5-M6 | 6.5 |
| MMU, cache semantics, interrupts/trace | M7-M9 | 6 |
| FPU | M10 | 4.5 |
| throughput/timing | M11 | 2 |
| **to reference parity** | **M0-M11** | **30.5** (assessment sections 4/8/9: 27-32 with caches deferred; consistent) |
| Amiga system bring-up | M12 | 3 (plus Paul's board time) |
| NetBSD bring-up | M13 | 3+ open-ended |
| optional split caches | M14 | 3, only if gated in |
| **total to end goal** | | **~37-40 + NetBSD overrun** |

The number that is least certain is M6 (precise faults and restart): it is the milestone where NetBSD, 68040.library and the manual all have an opinion, and the assessment (4.3) expected debugging there to be measured in weeks, not days.
